# Omarchy IPTV - Status Board

Owner: Project Manager. Living document; every role updates its own rows.
Task definitions, dependencies, gates: docs/PLAN.md. Last PM update:
2026-09-15 (defect-ledger reconciliation; the previous line still read
2026-09-12, over a board that had gained M2-03, M2-05, M2-09 and three
releases since).

States: todo | doing | blocked | review | done.
`done` requires evidence in the row or in Handoff notes (PLAN.md section 7).
Rule: FE does not start a task whose Deps (PLAN.md) are not all `done` here.

### M3 Two things that make a long list less hostile (docs/PLAN-M3.md)

| Task ID | Title | Owner | State | Evidence / Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M3-01 | Hide groups: `x` hides the cursor row's group, a HIDDEN section in the column, the same key brings it back (PLAN-M3 decisions 1-4, 7) | lead | built, live-verified, unreleased | f3f9593. Pure logic in `Model.js` (`hiddenGroupList`, `browsableChannels`, `hideAction`, the column's HIDDEN section), the python state mirror, one fixture for both readers, the fourth state whitelist taught on the day the key was added. 13 single-decision mutations each red; 1 python mutation red. 10,000 channels: 2.5 ms per scope rebuild with a group hidden, nothing when none is, All the SAME array by identity. Live: H1-H6 of `m3-scenario.sh`, 12/12 on three runs, including the real `x` through PanelKeyCatcher; 7/23 against v0.8.0 as the baseline. Ships in 0.9.0 |
| M3-02 | Audio and subtitle picker: `player tracks`, `t` opens a panel over the list, Enter selects, the player is authoritative (decisions 5, 6) | lead | built, live-verified, unreleased | 5c37177 and the paint-order fix after it (D-TRK-1). Helper verb tested over the socket against FakeMpv and a real token-carrying process (7 tests); `Model.parseTracks` / `trackRows` / `moveTrackCursor` / `trackAccessibleName` with 13 mutations red, one draft check rewritten after it survived one (rule 11). Live against a REAL mpv playing a two-audio, one-subtitle file: T1-T6, 18/18 on three runs, every switch confirmed by asking the player separately; the picker painted UNDER the list on the first run and no gate could have seen it. Found beside it: D-PLY-20. Ships in 0.9.0 |

Built by three lanes, integrated by the lead; 0 -> 227 on the frozen real inputs. The fourth repair, a per-source time offset, is deliberately NOT built: XMLTV carries its own offset, `parse_xmltv_time` honours it, and nothing here has been measured arriving wrong | done, unreleased |
| M4-02 | - | guide | **Programme detail.** The helper carries description, category and episode through instead of dropping them; `i` opens a panel over the list that renders only what `Model.programmeDetail` returns. Live-verified: title, time range, category, description and the next-programme line, on the real guide | done, unreleased |
| M4-04 | - | guide | **The keyboard map.** `?` opens an overlay built from the same tables the footer hints come from, so a key added to the product cannot be missing from the map. Six sections live. The milestone's one purely keyboard-first item, and the answer to a footer row that F-UX-2 measured as already overflowing | done, unreleased |
| M4-03 | - | guide | Hide individual channels. **Deferred to M5 by the product owner on 2026-10-02**, behind the channel-identity repair D-ID-4: it persists a per-channel reference, and four candidates sit inside that defect | deferred |

The user's own provider carries no channel numbers, so the feature shows the no-numbers message for them |
| M2-04 | Channel logos | - | todo (value in question) | Measured on 2026-09-15 across all four of the subscriber's own playlists (5,221 channels): **1,396 carry a non-empty `tvg-logo`, 27 percent.** By playlist: USChannels 737/3,335 (22 percent), SportsPPVAll 606/1,833 (33 percent), Kids 48/48, Default 5/5 - so the two lists they actually browse are the two with poor coverage, and the two with full coverage are 53 channels between them. **All 1,396 point at a single third-party host over https (`logo.m3uassets.com`)**, which means enabling logos sends that host a request per visible row, tied to this user's viewing, and puts an image cache on disk. That is the first outbound fetch in this plugin to anything but the user's own provider and EPG. PO decision needed on whether 27 percent coverage justifies the privacy cost, before any design work. If it goes ahead, the design has to lead with the four-in-five placeholder case, not the happy one |
| M2-05 | PiP via hyprctl float + pin | ARCH -> FE -> QA | done | released as v0.5.0 at db47c37. Integration found three rules implemented twice. Live pass on 5e3a374 (QA-RESULTS "M2-05 picture-in-picture LIVE pass"): zero P1, D-PIP-1 confirmed fixed on a real window, three new defects filed and all three fixed before the release (0d8f2ad, d3aece2, 043bebd). **D-PIP-3 remains open on its own terms per ruling PIP5**: the player window renders translucent because Omarchy's media-opacity exemption matches on class and ours does not match it |
| M2-06 | Recording via ffmpeg | - | todo | Large. Introduces a second long-lived child process with a different lifecycle from the player, writes files of unbounded size outside the cache, and needs its own failure surface. Not scoped |
| M2-07 | Catch-up / timeshift | - | **closed, no source exists** | Requires a provider that exposes catch-up. Checked on 2026-09-15: **none of the subscriber's four playlists carries a single `catchup`, `catchup-source`, `catchup-days`, `timeshift` or `tvg-rec` attribute** across 5,221 channels. We cannot verify one acceptance case against real data, and a feature this size built only against fixtures is how the v0.2.0 P1s happened. Blocked on evidence, not on effort. **CLOSED 2026-09-16 after phase 1**: the user probed their provider's panel directly and all three API requests returned the identical 748-byte HTML page, so the server is not parsing the query and there is no interface behind it. The M3U carries exactly four attribute keys across all 5,221 channels and none is a catch-up flag. There is no source of archive data anywhere for this subscriber, so this is not deferred, it is unbuildable. Reopen only if they change provider |
| M2-08 | First-class vertical bar layout | - | todo | The bar widget already renders in a vertical bar; this row is about making it good rather than making it work. Small, cosmetic, no blocker |

## Defects

Format: `<id> | severity | task | repro | state`. Severity is P1 to P4; P4
means diagnostic-only, visible to nobody but a maintainer reading a log.
State must NAME a state: open, fixed, verified fixed, accepted, superseded,
wont fix. `fixed` means a commit repairs it. `verified fixed` means QA re-ran
the original repro afterwards and said so, and the row cites where. A release
having shipped is not a state.

Ids are `D-` for a defect and `F-` for a finding. The distinction is tone, not
obligation, and `scripts/check-defect-ledger.py` treats them identically:
every id written into any tracked markdown file must have a row here carrying
a severity and a state, or `scripts/check.sh` is red (CLAUDE.md rule 13).

Recovers only on an external write (`omarchy bar set`, even of the identical value) or `omarchy restart shell`. Invisible to the dev harness, which supplies a fake `updateEntryInline` (QA-SOURCES section 6). `shots/E-back-t1.png`, `qs log`: `switch did not observe a cache load within 5000 ms` (QA-RESULTS L2, L6). Fixed at `845d445`: the service applies its own settings write locally (`Model.settingsWithOwnWrite` over `hostSettings`) and drops the override as soon as the host reports anything else; a `false` return from a writable entry is the host's `!dirty` branch and counts as success (`Model.barEntryWritable` separates a real persist failure). QA re-ran the original repro on the live shell: add is active in 723 ms, four Enter switches arrive in 145-161 ms with the guide, footer and `shell.json` all agreeing, and the second and third Enter on the same row report nothing (QA-RESULTS P2) | verified fixed |

Two suites call that shipping function for real: tests/Model.test.js:2045-2110 (the ladder as a table, walked pairwise and all-at-once, 54 checks, plus 7 named D-LIVE-22 checks) and tests/Model.spec.qml `test_footerPrecedenceCachedOverWarnings` in the Qt engine; both are floored with zero margin in scripts/check.sh (NODE_CHECKS_MIN 1427, QML_SPEC_MIN 68). The one join no test reaches -- Guide.qml `stale: root.serviceStatus === "cached"` -- was confirmed on a real screen by the defect report itself: QA-RESULTS O18 recorded `bannerKind` `playlistError`, which Guide.qml emits only when that same expression is true. So the input was right live and only the ranking was wrong. The four live passes that skipped this repro did not need to run it. |
| D-PLY-1 | P1 | M2-02 | PLY-LIFE-12 / PLY-RST-06 / PLY-SOCK-01, live shell, found 2026-09-14 at 8f9447e: after any helper-driven ladder-and-respawn the shell goes idle while the new player keeps playing. Two independent triggers, both deterministic: (a) `kill -STOP` the player and wait ~21 s for the health check's two-strike verdict (journal: `omarchy-iptv: mpv unresponsive, restarting player`), 3/3; (b) `rm "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/omarchy-iptv/mpv.sock"` then play another channel, 2/2. The relaunched mpv is genuinely playing (`idle-active` false, real `path`, window mapped and titled, its `user-data/omarchy-iptv` stash reading `playing:true` with the right id) while `status` reports `playing false`, `nowPlaying null`, `player.up/attached/wanted` all false. Because `wanted` is false the 250 ms retry timer is off, so it never reattaches (unchanged at 15 s and 30 s). Exactly one player exists throughout, so this is not a second-window defect. `stop` still reaches it and a shell restart reattaches to the same pid and restores the right state - but the interface already says stopped, so the user has no reason to try. The plan's P1 list verbatim: "the interface goes idle while the player keeps playing". Suspected: the `player restart --from term` reply path in `Service.qml` not re-arming `playerWanted` after a relaunch it issued itself. QA-RESULTS.md M8. **verified fixed at b16b479** (QA-RESULTS "M2-02 fix verification on b16b479" V2): neither trigger reproduces - A 3/3, B 2/2, shell stays `playing`/`attached`/`wanted` with one player and one window, unchanged at t+30 s. QA also counted the journal's relaunch line directly and found **exactly one relaunch per wedged player, never two**, because the harness check for that clause never runs (D-PLY-9) | verified fixed |

| D-PLY-4 | P2 | M2-02 | ARCH-P section 15's known startup race, gated and measured on 2026-09-14 at 8f9447e: **it reproduces, 14 losses in 30 trials (47%)**. Restart the shell and issue `play` from the earliest instant the successor's IPC accepts one (landed on the 3rd or 4th attempt in all 30 trials); the arriving state file then replaces `userState` wholesale and the session record the play just wrote is gone. Every loss reads `playing true`, `nowPlaying` correct, window mapped, `session` null. Consequence proven end to end: after a lost race the stream was killed with no shell attached and the reattaching shell marked nothing (`failedAt {}`) and raised no toast, so PO-3 is silently unavailable for roughly half the plays issued at shell start. Not fixed by QA on the lead's instruction; the "fix only if it reproduces" gate is met. QA-RESULTS.md M5. **verified fixed at b16b479**, re-measured with QA's own `race-trial.sh` from the 8f9447e pass (diffed: logic identical): **30 trials, 30 WON, 0 LOST, 0 VOID**, against 14 lost in 30 before. The default `--ytdl=no` is clean. The README's headline claim "No stream address, credential or header value ever reaches any command line" therefore has an unstated exception the README itself documents how to enable. | D-ID-2 | P3 | M1-06 state | **Accepted, measured limit of the D-ID-1 fix, not a defect in it.** A name key is refused when two channels share it, so a collision costs nothing today. But that is a single-snapshot property and a stable id exists only ACROSS snapshots: when a provider later removes one member of a colliding pair, the survivor becomes the unique holder and INHERITS the key, along with any favourite or recent that meant the row which went away. That is a silent WRONG channel, which is worse than a silent loss because the user cannot see it | open, accepted. Measured on the real lists: 26 colliding name groups on USChannels (55 rows, largest 3) and 39 on SportsPPVAll. Nobody has a second snapshot of these playlists over time, so the frequency is unmeasurable today and designing around it blind would be guessing. What would settle it: keep a dated copy of the user's playlists and re-measure name churn in a month. Recorded rather than designed around, and it is the reason the fix is described as raising survival from 1 of 3,335 to 3,271 rather than as solving identity. RE-CAUSED 2026-09-23, and the cause above is WRONG. Run against the shipping functions with `id` assigned the way assign_ids writes it, removing one member of a colliding pair does NOT hand the star to the survivor in either direction: remove the row the star did not mean and it follows the row it did (moved 1); remove the row it meant and it orphans (moved 0). The remap is keyed by the OLD id, which is url-derived, so it cannot reach another row. THE REAL SEQUENCE IS THREE STEPS and uses no remap at all: t0 the name is UNIQUE so the star is name-keyed; t1 a second channel takes the same name, both lose the key and the star ORPHANS at moved 0; t2 the starred row is the one removed, the survivor takes the key back and the orphan comes back to life on it. The stored id never changes -- it is the same string at t0 and t2 -- so nothing moved the star, an orphaned key simply became valid again pointing elsewhere. The middle step is the mechanism and the filed version omits it: a star made DURING a collision is url-keyed and never at risk. Both writers agree at every step, checked by running channelIds and assign_ids over the same three lists. CONSEQUENCE: the open question this row records -- measure name churn over a month -- decides nothing and is retired. The frequency is not what makes the star wrong; one saved key cannot identify a channel and a key that stops resolving is kept rather than cleaned up, which is D-ID-4's finding. Matching on the url key first makes t2 orphan instead of relocate, so D-ID-2 AND D-ID-4 HAVE ONE FIX, already accepted and costed there. POPULATION, measured on the maintainer's own state file read-only: 8 of 8 saved references are `t:` keyed and 0 are `n:`, so the exposure on the configured list is zero. Still accepted, on different grounds: it waits on D-ID-4's schema change, not on data. Three node checks and three python tests, six mutations all red. docs/QA-RESULTS.md 2026-09-23 |
| D-A11Y-1 | P3 | M2-01 Sources | Latent credential exposure on a sink CLAUDE.md rule 5 does not list. `Accessible.description` is correctly masked (`Guide.qml:3172`) but Qt derives an editable field's accessible **Value** from the control's `text`, which rule 5 never considered. `fieldDisplay` (`Guide.qml:1626`) returns the masked string only while the field IS masked, so a revealed playlist or EPG URL publishes its credentials in full, and the Xtream **server** and **username** are not maskable at all so they publish raw always. The comment at `Guide.qml:3171` says a screen reader never gets the query; that is true of the description and false of the value. Unobservable today only because D-GS-3 means nothing is published at all, so this becomes live the moment Quickshell is fixed | **open, half shipped.** Shipped under PO ruling AX1, all pure gain: the false comment corrected, `Accessible.passwordEdit` deleted (inert in both directions on Qt 6.11.2, measured twice by different toolchains, and it left the password with NO label at all, which is also fixed), docs/UX-SOURCES.md 6.7 item 7 and the password row corrected, and CLAUDE.md rule 5 given the correct two-part statement plus the text-event payload as a named sink. **The masking itself was REFUSED under ruling AX2**: the proposed one-line edit was measured to break the Xtream login form outright, a user typing `joe` gets a field holding `e`, because `revealed` is only ever set for URL fields and a masked field is treated as fully selected on every keystroke. Done properly it is five `Model.js` sites plus a UX regression, since a fixed-length mask hides whether the field is even filled while the password below shows true-length bullets. Live risk is zero until D-GS-3 lands upstream, so it is scheduled to that trigger TRIPWIRE INSTALLED 2026-09-23, and the masking is still refused -- ruling AX2 stands. What was missing was not the fix but the TRIGGER: the deferral rests on the premise that no Quickshell window publishes a tree, and that premise was a comment in three files and an assertion in NONE. Two things joined by a name, with nothing verifying the join, which is rule 13 exactly -- and the thing on the other side of this particular join is provider credentials on the session bus. `tests/test_a11y_premise.py` now pins the platform the premise was measured on (quickshell 0.3.1-1, qt6-base 6.11.2-3, at-spi2-core 2.60.6-1, from docs/ACCESSIBILITY-INVESTIGATION.md section 6) and goes red the moment any of them moves. It does NOT claim to detect the upstream fix; it detects that the EVIDENCE HAS EXPIRED, which is the cheap and honest thing to detect, and its failure message says what becomes live and what to re-run rather than reporting a version diff. It skips rather than passes where the versions cannot be read, because a guard that passes having learned nothing is the kind this replaces. Proven red by moving a pinned version. **Trigger sharpened 2026-09-25**: the upstream fix is now a verified commit in an unreleased Quickshell (`916a0dd`, 2026-08-28; D-GS-3 row and investigation section 10), so the fixture names it and `onRed` says to check the new build for it BEFORE re-probing. The tripwire still detects expiry rather than the fix -- that stays the honest thing to detect -- but the reader no longer has to re-derive which commit matters. Asserted, not just written: the test now requires `916a0dd` in both `upstream` and `onRed`, proven red by dropping it from the fixture. Ruling AX2 still stands and the masking is still refused; live risk is still zero. |

| D-GS-3 | P3 | M2-09 | Empty accessibility tree: with the guide open on 3,335 rows the shell's AT-SPI root (`quickshell`, role application) reports `ChildCount 0` and `GetChildren` empty, both with toolkit-accessibility enabled at runtime and enabled from startup, while a GTK client on the same bus in the same moment reports a child. No screen reader can announce any row, failure state or `row N of M`. Detail QA-RESULTS M2-09 section 6 | **cause settled 2026-09-15, not ours** (docs/ACCESSIBILITY-INVESTIGATION.md section 6). Measured with the gate open, selecting the connection by `ToolkitName` rather than by name, and confirming window mapping from the compositor: a plain Qt 6.11.2 QML window publishes `ChildCount 1`, a Quickshell `PanelWindow` publishes 0, and a Quickshell `FloatingWindow`, an ordinary xdg-toplevel, also publishes 0. So Qt's bridge, the bus and the gate are all fine, the break is Quickshell, and it is NOT specific to layer-shell surfaces as everyone including this project assumed. The reproduction contains none of our code. **Both upstream reports filed 2026-09-15**: Quickshell https://github.com/quickshell-mirror/quickshell/issues/1144 (the cause) and Omarchy https://github.com/omacom/omarchy/issues/12009 (the host sets no roles or names at all, a separate and larger problem). Stays `open` on our board, tracking the Quickshell issue, because the plugin's accessibility stays unverifiable until that lands. GS5 stays open. The running shell was never restarted and the gate was restored. **Upstream resolution 2026-09-25 (docs/ACCESSIBILITY-INVESTIGATION.md section 10): confirmed, fixed, unreleased.** Our report #1144 was closed 2026-09-21 with zero comments, so nothing here learned why; the answer came as a third-party comment on the Omarchy report that reproduced the defect independently and agreed on section 6.2 point 3, the counterintuitive half. Verified against the GitHub API rather than taken from the mail: the fix is `quickshell-mirror/quickshell@916a0dd`, authored **2026-08-28**, whose own message names the mechanism -- \"Accessibility hooks created by QQuick module init are destroyed by the QApplication destructor and not recreated by a new QApplication\". That explains every section 6 measurement at once and supersedes section 4.5's `Qt::Popup` hypothesis: window type was never relevant, the hooks were torn down at launch before any window existed, which is why probing window types could not find it. The fix predates our filing by eighteen days. **Not released**: latest is v0.3.1, published 2026-08-21. The cited PR number 404s on the mirror and is recorded as unconfirmed. Still `open` here: installed is `quickshell 0.3.1-1`, everything the guide declares is still inert, and the trigger is now a known commit rather than an unknown date |

Detail QA-RESULTS GATE pass, "A new defect the gate was not looking for" | fixed before it could ship: ruling PIP11 at 6f084ca replaced exit-status detection with a compositor readback, and the built code defines success in exactly one place, `Model.pipVerify` (Service.qml:1030 and :1045). | D-PIP-3 | P3 | M2-05 | The player window renders at `0.985 / 0.96` because `--wayland-app-id=omarchy-iptv` misses Omarchy's media-opacity exemption, which matches on class; confirmed by reading the window tags and `hyprctl getprop ... opaque` returning false. Detail QA-RESULTS GATE pass G-11 | open. Rulings PIP5 and PIP13 keep it as its own defect to be fixed on its own terms; no commit changes the class or applies the exemption. README 34ebf80 only describes the translucency and points at `contrib/windows.lua`, an optional rule the user must paste by hand. QA's last word, the 5e3a374 live-pass verdict, is that it remains open on its own terms per PIP5 MEASURED AND ACCEPTED 2026-09-23: there is no plugin-side remedy, and the three that looked plausible were each tried against the live compositor rather than reasoned about. The mechanism, read out of `/usr/share/omarchy/default/hypr/windows.lua`: `o.window(".*", { tag = "+default-opacity" })` tags every window, `o.window({ tag = "default-opacity" }, { opacity = "0.985 0.96" })` dims anything carrying it, and an application opts out with a RULE in its own `apps/<name>.lua`, the way davinci-resolve and hermes do. Dead end 1: `hyprctl dispatch ...tag({ tag = "-default-opacity" })` on our own window by address. The compositor answers `ok` and the tag is UNCHANGED -- it renders as `default-opacity*`, and the asterisk is a rule-applied tag that a dispatch cannot remove. `opaque` stays false. This is PIP11's lesson arriving in a new place: rc and `ok` are not evidence, and a plugin-side fix was written, tested green and reverted because the LIVE check caught that it changed nothing. Dead end 2: `hyprctl setprop <addr>` with alpha, alphaoverride, alphainactive or opaque -- every spelling answers `unknown request`. Dead end 3: `hyprctl keyword windowrule ...` -- refused outright with `keyword can't work with non-legacy parsers. Use eval.` So the only lever is a window rule, and a window rule lives in configuration this plugin may not write (CLAUDE.md never-touch). The remedy is the one line already in `contrib/windows.lua`, and the README points at it three times (installation list, the float note, and the troubleshooting entry that names the cause). Accepted at 0.985/0.96 for anyone who has not pasted it. The real fix is upstream -- one `apps/omarchy-iptv.lua` in Omarchy, exactly like the two that already exist -- and is not filed. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, D-PIP-3 2026-09-23 FOURTH DEAD END, and it was the one the roadmap flagged and my pass missed. `/usr/share/omarchy/default/hypr/apps/pip.lua` tags any window whose TITLE matches `(Picture.?in.?[Pp]icture)` and then removes `default-opacity` and sets `opacity = "1 1"`. The helper already writes the window title over IPC on every channel change, so this looked like one line. It is loaded -- `windows.lua:22` requires all of `apps/`, and the default-opacity dim is deliberately applied AFTER apps get their chance to opt out -- so the rule is live. It still never fires for us, tested both ways on 2026-09-23. Changing the title at RUNTIME over the mpv socket: title became `Harness Live Picture in Picture`, tags stayed `default-opacity*`, no `pip` tag, `opaque` false. Launching a probe mpv with the matching title ALREADY SET at spawn: same, plus not floating, not pinned, and 645x718 rather than pip.lua's 600x338, so none of that rule's block applied. The likeliest mechanism is that a Wayland toplevel maps before its title is set, so the rule evaluates against a title that does not match yet -- but the result is empirical either way and does not depend on the explanation. So the conclusion stands and is now better tested: four levers, all measured, none available to a plugin. What was wrong in the cell above was the word ONLY -- it said the only lever is a window rule, when a title-matching rule was sitting unexamined in the roadmap the whole time. |

| D-ID-3 | P2 | id scheme | **The shell undoes the helper's id migration on the same fetch.** Found 2026-09-21 by observing the running plugin headless (docs/QA-HEADLESS-2026-09-21.md section 1); independently reproduced by the verifier. On the qa-id-rotate fixture with the seeded scheme-1 state: the helper (argv with `--state-dir`) logs `moved 8` and rewrites state.json onto scheme 2; then `handlePlaylistExit -> applyPlaylistStatus -> adoptSourceStats -> saveState()` writes the state the service loaded BEFORE the helper ran back over it -- the file (0600, later mtime) holds the seed's u: ids plus the service's own fields (cacheLayout 2, session null, fetchedAt). `ipc refresh` logs `moved 8` AGAIN, proving the revert; favorites resolve 4 rows where the remapped state resolves 7. Service.qml contains no state reload after a fetch and zero references to `Model.remapChannelIds` / `remapStateIds` -- the function phase 2 lifted for exactly this call site was never called. Present since the migration shipped; in 0.7.1 and 0.7.2, whose changelogs claim favorites survive a credential change: on a list with u: ids they do not. tests/fixtures/qa-id-rotate could not see it because it drives the helper alone; this is the class two review rounds refused, caught only at the sink. Fix: when a channel set is applied (after `channelsFile.reload()`), run `Model.remapChannelIds(root.userState, channels)` and, if `moved` is non-zero, adopt the state and save -- idempotent with the helper's write; prove it headless: favorites resolve 7 after the first fetch, no second `moved` on refresh, state.json byte-stable across refreshes | fixed 2026-09-21 in Service.qml, proven at the sink headless, independently verified. `applyChannels` -- the one place a channel set is applied to memory -- now carries a scheme-1 -> scheme-2 map per parsed list (`Model.channelIdRemap`, cached on the LRU entry because the composition costs tens of milliseconds per call on 10,000 rows and applyChannels runs on every switch inside the 150 ms budget) and `adoptIdScheme()` moves the in-memory state onto it (`Model.remapStateIds`), writing only on a move. The service's writes now agree with the helper's and the order of the two writers stops mattering; idempotent, no second write. Proven by `scripts/dev-harness/id-rotate-scenario.sh` (new, IPC-only, its check-tree half now in `check.sh`): against dev's Service.qml the Favorites scope resolves 4 by name and `refresh` logs a SECOND move; against the fix it resolves 7 with the merged favourite once, state.json holds the seven migrated ids at 0600, exactly ONE `moved` line after the first fetch and still one after `refresh`, the file byte-stable across the refresh but for sources[].fetchedAt, and after the switch to list-v2 five favourites resolve by name with the two D-ID-2 rows orphaned as predicted. The verifier refuted the lane's first proof artefact: two assertions had encoded one side of a load-time race (Model.stateOnLoad's in-flight replay versus the PO-3 clear of a seeded session), so the seed is now installed without its session record and the expectations are the fixture README's own table; the helper logs `moved 7` there and the invariant is the count of `moved` lines, not its number. Integrator's own re-run, headless, after the race fix: 37 passed / 0 failed on the integrated tree (recents ['n:92937b2e','t:india.test','n:320e63f7'], lastPlayed n:9ca3981a, exactly one move across the refresh, five by name on list-v2); 23 / 14 against dev's Service.qml (favorites=4, a second move, the seed's u: ids on disk) |

| F-HARNESS-1 | P4 | dev harness | Under the harness's headless floating-window mode, the FIRST wtype keystroke into a fresh shell is intermittently lost (1 of 4 fresh shells in verification: `sky` arrived as `ky`), and a row-count assertion cannot tell the two apart (both filter 20 rows to 3). Found by the harness-mode verifier. Rule for headless keystroke scenarios: assert the exact query string read back over IPC, never the row count; send a throwaway key or reset with `ipc query ""` and retry once before grading. The README sentence attributing the loss to an id-shadowing bug was wrong and is withdrawn | fixed. Under the harness's floating-window mode the FIRST wtype keystroke into a fresh shell is intermittently lost: 1 of 4 fresh shells in one verification, 2 of 2 in the integration lane, 1 of 2 in its verifier (`sky` arrives as `ky`), and a row-count assertion cannot tell the two apart. Rule for headless keystroke scenarios, now in scripts/dev-harness/README.md: assert the exact query string read back over IPC, never the row count; send a throwaway key or reset with `ipc query ""` and retry once before grading. Cause unknown; the earlier attribution to an id-shadowing bug was wrong and is withdrawn -- Under real layer-shell focus (the dev harness on the live display) the first keystroke arrived whole, 1 of 1; the loss is so far seen only in the floating mode under cage. FIXED 2026-09-23, and the rule prescribed for this turned out to be UNIMPLEMENTABLE as written. It said to assert the exact query string read back over IPC -- and no IPC verb read it back. `numberState` carries a field called `query`, which is presumably why the rule looked satisfiable, but it is the PRE-ENTRY snapshot that Esc and Backspace restore: with the search set to `sky` it reads EMPTY while the cursor sits on Sky Sports Main Event. Every scenario following the rule would have compared against the wrong string and passed regardless. Three changes: `queryLive` added to the harness snapshot, named as `cursorIndexLive` beside it already is, so the live text can be read at all; `run.sh key` primes a fresh shell once with a Shift press and release, which reaches the surface without changing any state, keyed to the pid file `start` rewrites so a new shell primes again; and `run.sh type <text>` types and then PROVES the text arrived, retrying once and failing loudly on the second miss. The compensation lives in the harness now rather than in prose every scenario author has to remember -- a rule joined to its callers by nothing is rule 13's shape, and here it was not merely unenforced but impossible. Verified live: `type sky` lands on attempt 1 with `queryLive` reading `sky`; with no shell running it reports both attempts, says this is not the first-keystroke race, and exits 1. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, F-HARNESS-1 2026-09-23 |
| D-CHNO-6 | P4 | channel numbers | `QML MouseArea at Guide.qml:2730 (dev): Cannot anchor to an item that isn't a parent or sibling` -- the lead-slot favourite hit target (M2-03 4.2) has `anchors.left: lead.left` where `lead` is neither its parent nor a sibling. Pre-existing; fires once per run on dev because the guide never mapped headless, and once per rendered row under the harness's floating-window mode (16 to 96 lines a run), so it will fire per row on the live shell too. The anchor is refused by Qt, so the hit target's left edge is whatever the default gives. Found by the harness-mode verifier. Fix: anchor to the row's own edge or reparent the target; verify with the floating mode that the warning count drops to zero FIXED 2026-09-22, and it was NOT cosmetic. Qt refused the anchor, so `x` fell back to 0 and the hit target started at the ROW's left edge rather than the lead slot's - covering the number column, which is precisely what the anchor was written to prevent. On a numbered playlist a click on a channel's NUMBER therefore toggled its favorite. (That consequence is read from the code: an anchor Qt refuses leaves x at its default, and the target's width is max(space(28), space(12)+leadWidth) = space(36) from x=0. It was not clicked on screen - the harness has no pointer injection - so it is stated as what the code does, not as an observed click.) The cause is a generation gap: `lead` is a grandchild of the row delegate and the MouseArea is a child, so they are uncle and nephew. Fixed by binding x across it - rowContent.x + lead.x - space(12), written that way rather than the algebraically equal lead.x so it still tracks either margin. Proven headless, same recipe both ways with the log truncated so the count is one run: 13 warnings before, 0 after. | verified fixed |
| D-RUNG-13 | P3 | contrast | **The cursor row has lost its accent on every theme, not the eight the design costed.** Found on screen 2026-09-21 (D-RUNG-7's probe): the rendered cursor-row ink is the text token byte-for-byte on rose-pine, tokyo-night and catppuccin. Cause: `Color.menu.selectedBackground` is `Util.alpha(foreground, 0.08)`, an uncomposited colour with alpha, and `Model.qmlRgb` drops the alpha, so `Model.cursorInk` receives the TEXT colour as its fill and its loop falls through to text on every theme; the '15 of 23 byte-identical' figure in the SG4 costing is false on screen. Direction safe (5.83 / 6.86 / 9.13 against the fill), so nothing is unreadable; the accent the ruling chose is simply never shown. Fix: composite the fill over `Color.menu.background` when its alpha is under 1 before handing it to cursorInk, or pass alpha and background into `cursorInkHex`; node test with an alpha fill. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-21 section 4 FIXED 2026-09-21. The fill is composited inside Model.js before the comparison (`qmlFill`, new fourth argument `background` on `cursorInkHex`), and the false comment that asserted the fill arrives pre-composited is gone - that sentence WAS the defect in prose. But the ruling that followed is the substantive part: the corrected ink is NOT applied to the cursor row. PO ruling 2026-09-21 - the 2 px mark means CURSOR, the accent means ACTIVE - so the cursor row keeps the plain text token and `cursorInk` now has exactly two consumers, the selected group label and the ConfirmDialog's selected button. Inking the cursor row instead would have cost contrast on 22 of 23 themes, median 31 per cent and up to 73 (white 17.55 to 4.78), left the selected name fainter than an unselected one on 23 of 23, and bought nothing on white, vantablack and solitude, whose accents are grey (saturation 0.00, 0.00, 0.10). That costing was produced by a refuter after the UX panel recommended the opposite on a floor figure that hid it. | verified fixed |
| D-RUNG-14 | P3 | contrast | **The 0.7 caption rung is under 4.5:1 on tokyo-night on screen, where the model passes it.** Rendered 2026-09-21: group count 4.12, footer status 4.25, footer verbs 4.30, Sources count 4.12 against a model value of 4.64. Because 10 px regular captions render 11 to 13 per cent below the model (F-CAL-1), any caption site the model puts under about 5.1 fails on a real screen, so ruling SG2's '6 of 23 under' is understated. Product question: raise the caption rung again, make captions bold (bold 10 px renders at model accuracy, D-RUNG-9), or accept and say so. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-21 sections 1 and 5 FIXED 2026-09-21 by PO ruling: the four sites are BOLD, not raised. 10 px regular renders 11 to 13 per cent below the model and 10 px bold renders at model accuracy (F-CAL-1), so bold recovers the shortfall without touching the 0.7 rung, which is what keeps secondary text secondary; `monospace` resolves to JetBrainsMono Nerd Font, which ships a real Bold face, and monospace bold has the same advance width so nothing reflows. UNVERIFIED on our own sites until one is captured - the bold-caption class still has zero fixture rows and rests on a host header measurement (rule 14), so this row stays honest about that. Two further 0.7 captions share the rung and were never measured, the Xtream prose and the first-run terminal caption; they are prose, were left regular, and are recorded here rather than silently claimed. SUPERSEDED IN PART by F-CAL-3: the verification below was done under headless cage and does NOT hold on the live display, where the same four sites read 5.45 to 5.86 - the regular-weight values. The bold ships and is correct; the claim that it recovers the shortfall is withdrawn until re-measured live. VERIFIED on screen 2026-09-21 under headless cage, on our own sites for the first time: group count and footer status -0.0007, the UK-ENTERTAINMENT and Sources counts -0.054, against a model of 6.2384 - where the SAME sites at regular weight measured -0.79. The bold-caption class now has four rows of ours in the calibration fixture instead of none. REOPENED 2026-09-22 by F-CAL-3, having been verified first in the wrong environment and then, when finally measured live, on the wrong theme. The bold ships and stays -- it costs nothing, it keeps secondary text secondary where raising the rung would not, and it is right in principle -- but it recovers nothing on a real screen: the four sites measure 5.4475 to 5.6956 live against a model of 6.2384, which are the regular-weight values to two decimals. And catppuccin, the theme the live pass measured, read 5.45 BEFORE any change; the themes actually under 4.5 live at the 0.7 rung are rose-pine (2.96 to 3.15) and tokyo-night (4.12 to 4.30), and neither has ever been measured bold. So the defect this row describes is still on the screen on 2 of the 3 themes ever measured live, and the remedy is unchosen. What is known now and was not: the loss is glyph stroke coverage, so no opacity arithmetic recovers it and raising the rung is the only remedy the model can price -- at the cost in hierarchy `S x H = F` makes explicit. Next, and cheap: measure tokyo-night live at bold before choosing anything, since it is the failing theme and the question may settle there. tests/Model.test.js derives the themes still owed that measurement from the fixture and goes red when one is taken, so this cannot be forgotten the way it was. RESTATED 2026-09-22 after the first live measurement of the bold that was not confounded by a stale build. Both previous claims about this row were wrong. 'Bold recovers the shortfall' was verified against a build under cage and never on a screen; 'bold recovers nothing anyone can see' was measured on a shell running pre-bold code. What bold actually does is SITE-DEPENDENT, and the remedy question turns on that. On the CARD fill it does what was claimed: tokyo-night footer status measures 4.6410 against a model of 4.6433, the composite byte-exact, where regular weight on the same theme measured 4.25 -- under AA to over it. On the CURSOR fill it cannot: the selected tint lifts the background, the model is 4.2157, bold measures 4.1893 which is model accuracy, and no weight reaches 4.5 from there. On rose-pine the rung models 3.3446 on the card and 3.1402 on the cursor, under AA before a pixel is drawn. So this row is NOT closed: it is fixed on the card fill, arithmetically unreachable on the cursor fill, and unreachable on rose-pine at any weight. Two further cautions, both real. The tokyo margin is 0.1410, smaller than the fixture's own 0.15 tolerance and about two quantisation steps. And Model.js SECTION_HEADER_FLOOR is 4.65, citing this very defect, so the shipping code already grades 4.6433 a fail for this size class one file away -- that inconsistency needs settling before anything here is called done. Next: decide the remedy for the cursor row and rose-pine, where weight is not one. FIXED 2026-09-23 by making the rung an OUTPUT of the fill rather than a constant, `Model.captionAlpha(foreground, fill, floor)`, which is the same shape `sectionHeaderAlpha` already used for D-RUNG-9 and returns byte-identically to it on all four raised card surfaces. The product-owner call was to raise the rung on the cursor row. Result: 0 of 46 theme surfaces under the 4.65 floor, where a flat 0.7 left 6 of 23 under 4.5 on the selection and 3 of 23 on the card. It is surgical -- 16 of 23 themes get 0.7 back byte-identical, 11 of 46 surfaces rise, worst 0.89 on rose-pine's selection. THE COST, measured and ACCEPTED by the product owner rather than discovered later: on the selected group row the count now sits almost exactly level with its own label, because both then target about 4.7 on the same fill. In CIE L* the step falls from 10.20 to 0.15 on catppuccin-latte and 11.36 to 0.38 on rose-pine; hierarchy there is carried by size, weight, position and hue instead of lightness. No theme is NEWLY inverted -- 9 of 23 already read the count louder than the label at the flat rung, D-RUNG-15's residue -- and a 4.55 floor was priced and rejected, moving the worst step only to 0.63. Three defects in the first cut were found by an adversarial pass and not by me: the Sources count was left on the card rung while its hover fill lifts the surface exactly as the selection does, excused by a comment claiming `Style.hoverFillFor` was not modellable, which was false and took one look to check; contrast is NOT monotonic in alpha, so a short-circuit on the full-opacity value returned a FAILING rung where a passing one existed two steps away; and the two deliberately-regular prose sites were handed the BOLD floor, which renders about 11 per cent lower. All three fixed. NOT verified on a screen, deliberately unclaimed: F-CAL-4 is one day old and a live confirmation on a CONFIRMED BUILD is owed before this says verified. VERIFIED ON SCREEN 2026-09-23, on a build CONFIRMED LOADED before a pixel was read -- the process rule F-CAL-4 produced, and the first measurement in this project taken under it. Dev harness in layer mode (the production window type) on the live display, each theme rendered from a scratch HOME so the user's own theme was never switched; state, cache, settings and theme all sha256-identical at the end; every frame taken to stdout and parsed in memory, so no capture of the desktop was written. The count on the SELECTED row, the site no weight could fix, on the two themes that actually failed: tokyo-night 4.1893 to 4.6489, rose-pine 3.14 modelled to 4.6508. The footer on the card: tokyo-night 4.6410 to 4.7093, rose-pine 3.05 measured on 2026-09-21 to 4.6167. All four clear 4.5. Stated honestly rather than rounded up: all four land marginally UNDER the 4.65 floor, by 0.0011 to 0.0445, which is at or inside this method's one-LSB resolution of about 0.05, so the measurement cannot distinguish just-under from just-over at the floor. What it distinguishes easily is the move, which is 0.46 on tokyo-night and 1.51 on rose-pine, ten to thirty times that resolution. The four rows are in the calibration fixture with their env, their build and the background they actually measured. | verified fixed |
| D-RUNG-16 | P4 | contrast | **`sectionHeaderAlpha` carries the non-monotonicity bug `captionAlpha` was just fixed for.** `Model.js` short-circuits with `if (full <= target) return 1`, which assumes contrast rises with alpha. It does not: `colorOver` blends in gamma space and the luminance transfer is convex, so when the channels move in opposite directions the curve peaks in the INTERIOR. Demonstrated on `captionAlpha` before its fix with foreground `#c50236` over fill `#20f91e` -- 0.70 gives 4.3973, 0.83 peaks at 4.7318, 1.00 falls to 4.2580 -- so the guard returns a rung that FAILS the floor while a passing one exists two steps away. LATENT, not live, which is the only reason this is a P4: the minimum full-opacity contrast across all 46 installed surfaces is 5.9384, 28 per cent above the floor, and a 10,000-step scan finds no non-monotonic surface among them. The branch is also dead -- deleting it leaves the suite green and the function byte-identical on every installed surface -- which is exactly why nothing caught it. The fix is the same one applied to `captionAlpha`: drop the short circuit, and fall back to the alpha that MAXIMISES contrast rather than to 1, since the counterexample shows full opacity can be the worst rung available. Not done here because it changes section-header rendering and this change had a different brief. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, caption rung 2026-09-23 FIXED 2026-09-23, and the fix is that the search now exists ONCE. The loop was written twice and only the newer copy was audited, which is how a defect outlived its own discovery by a day; `alphaForContrast` is now the single implementation and both `sectionHeaderAlpha` and `captionAlpha` call it, asserted by RESULT over all 23 themes rather than by a comment. The short circuit is gone and the fallback returns the alpha that MAXIMISES contrast rather than 1, because the counterexample shows full opacity can be the worst rung available. Proven invisible where it matters: both rungs, both surfaces, all 23 installed themes, byte-identical before and after, pinned by value so a future drift names itself. Four mutations, each red -- including the short circuit restored exactly as it shipped. Marked verified WITHOUT a screen deliberately, and the reason is not haste: the pins prove the output is byte-identical on every installed theme, so there is nothing a screen could show. The only behaviour that moved is on inputs no theme produces. | verified fixed |
| D-RUNG-17 | P3 | contrast | **`cursorInk`'s fallback carried the same defect, and worse.** Found while fixing D-RUNG-16 by asking whether the third search in this family shared the assumption. It does: `colorMix` interpolates in gamma space and `relativeLuminance` is convex, so contrast against a third colour is not monotonic in the MIX fraction either, and returning the far endpoint when nothing clears the target picks an arbitrary point rather than the best one. Measured over a deterministic 20,000-pair sweep: a better mix existed on **57.7 per cent** of the fallbacks, worst case returning 1.0273 where 4.5722 was available one step in -- a 3.5 ratio-point loss, an order of magnitude worse than D-RUNG-16's. LATENT: 0 of 23 installed themes reach the fallback, which is precisely why neither this nor its sibling was ever noticed. FIXED by `mixForContrast`, the mix-search sibling of `alphaForContrast`, seeded with the unmixed colour so it beats even the sweep that found the defect. The shipping ink is byte-identical on all 23 themes, pinned. Three mutations, each red. One consequence worth recording: this turned the D-RUNG-13 pin red, because that pin had recorded the exact wrong answer the unsafe seam produced -- 'collapses to the text token' -- as if it were the property. It was a SYMPTOM of one fallback. Restated to the real property and made sharper in the process: the themes that mix and the themes the unsafe path gets wrong are now derived independently and asserted to be THE SAME SET, which they are, all 8 of them. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, caption rung 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| D-RUNG-15 | P3 | contrast | **The selected GROUP label inked with the raw accent, under 4.5:1 on 8 of 23 themes, and no gate ever reported it.** Found 2026-09-21 by the raw-accent inventory that replaced a gate counting spellings. The label sits ON the selected fill (Guide.qml group delegate), not on the card, so the bar is 4.5:1 against the fill: floor 2.7969 rose-pine, then miasma and catppuccin-latte. D-RUNG-4 corrected the channel name and never looked at this column, and the old gate matched `hasCursor ? root.selectedText` only, so a plain `groupRow.selected ? root.selectedText` was invisible to it - rule 14's failure mode inside the check written to prevent it. FIXED the same day by pointing it at `Model.cursorInkHex`, the function written for exactly this case and never aimed at it: 0 of 23 under 4.5, floor 4.7025, and the accent survives untouched on the 15 themes that already cleared the target. This is also where the corrected D-RUNG-13 arithmetic earns its keep. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-21 section 4 | verified fixed |
| F-CAL-1 | P3 | contrast | **The contrast model's error is size-dependent, and the fixture's single tolerance hid it.** Calibration top-up 2026-09-21, 17 rows on rose-pine, tokyo-night and catppuccin: 11-14 px text within 0.10 of the model, the 28 px glyph and the bar glyph within 0.03 (two shipped sites between alpha 0.6 and 0.9 among them), but every 10 px regular-weight caption 0.17 to 0.79 below it, 11 to 13 per cent, while bold 10 px does not lose it. Six appended rows exceed maxAbsError 0.15 and node's calibration checks went 1434/0 to 1434/2, one of them on SHAPE because its expectation was a hard-coded six-element array. Ruling: rows carry a size class; captions get a relative tolerance of 15 per cent, everything else keeps 0.15 absolute; the test derives its expectations from the rows. Reported, not adjusted, by the pass; applied by the lead. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-21 section 1 Residual, ruled the same day: three cursor-fill text rows (tokyo-night cursor 0.52, catppuccin cursor 1.0 and 0.52) stay 0.17 to 0.25 off, beyond the text class, and are pinned by name rather than covered by a tolerance; that residual is F-CAL-2. | fixed |
| F-CAL-2 | P3 | contrast | **Three cursor-fill text rows miss the text tolerance, and no pass yet can say whether that is the surface or the string.** tokyo-night cursor 0.52 off 0.17, catppuccin cursor 1.0 off 0.25 and catppuccin cursor 0.52 off 0.23 against the text class's 0.15 absolute (F-CAL-1). The model's arithmetic is not at fault: the fill measures as modelled and the rendered ink is the text token less a few units of peak coverage (QA-RESULTS section 4). The cursor side loses more than the card on five of the six themes in the fixture and the same on retropc (0.12 on both), but every pass so far measured a different channel name on each surface (row 1 under the cursor, row 2 beside it), so a surface effect is not separated from the coverage of those particular glyphs; the row delegate carries no transform, Behavior or animation (Guide.qml:2480-2600). Ruling: the three rows carry knownDeviation F-CAL-2 in tests/fixtures/contrast-calibration.json and tests/Model.test.js holds them strictly - the pinned set is asserted by name, an unpinned row must hold, and a pinned row that holds goes red so the pin comes off in the same change; proven by mutation, 1438/0 as written. No tolerance widened. Next: one live pass measures ONE string on both surfaces by moving the cursor one row, catppuccin first (largest residual). docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-21 section 1 RESOLVED 2026-09-21 under headless cage, and it was not a surface effect at all. Measured with the SAME STRING on both surfaces for the first time by moving the cursor one row between two frames: BBC One HD reads -0.2075 on the card and -0.1991 on the fill, a difference of 0.008. The error tracks the STRING, not the surface: eight 14 px names on one surface, one theme, one model value of 11.3411, measured 11.0372 to 11.3001 - a spread of 0.041 to 0.304, because the peak-pixel method under-reads until some pixel is fully covered and a longer string reaches that sooner. That single mechanism also explains why bold and glyphs read at model accuracy while 10 px regular does not. Separately proven: the model is EXACT given the fill Qt really paints - its only arithmetic error is a one-unit rounding in the composite (rose-pine model ede7e4 vs rendered ede8e4, worth 0.039; catppuccin matches byte-for-byte), and against the rendered fill the measured 5.9772 and 9.3561 equal the model's 5.9772 and 9.3561 to four decimals. WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS A METHOD RULING, not arithmetic: record the string on every calibration row, always measure the longest available string, or widen the text class - because a card row misses the 0.15 text tolerance just as a cursor row does, and both are now pinned to show it. METHOD RULED by the product owner 2026-09-21: measure the LONGEST available string at each site, because it is closest to the true rendered token, and RECORD the string on every row so a figure can be reproduced. The text class keeps 0.15 absolute and the pins come off as rows are re-measured under that method. Rows carrying a site but measured under the old method are the ones still owed a re-measure; the fixture's note carries the rule. RE-MEASURE DONE 2026-09-22, headless, and the ruling is vindicated: all 16 text rows on the four re-measurable themes were re-measured on the harness fixture's 89-character channel name, the SAME string on both surfaces of the same row, and EVERY one lands inside the text class's 0.15 absolute - worst 0.1359 on catppuccin's card detail line. Every knownDeviation pin is therefore gone and the fixture asserts that none remains. Two things this turned up. retropc COULD NOT be re-measured: ~/.config/omarchy/themes/retropc ships no colors.toml, so the shell falls back to #cacccc on #101315 and renders a palette that is not retropc; the attempt produced fallback values and was discarded, and its old rows stand unverified. And the optimism invariant BROKE honestly - rose-pine's cursor name measures 5.9471 against a model 5.9384, 0.0087 ABOVE it - because with a long string the coverage bias nearly vanishes and what is left is the model's one-unit composite rounding, which rounds either way. The invariant was restated rather than widened: no sample may exceed its model by more than 0.05, and the mean error must stay negative. Both are stricter than what they replaced - a sign flip now trips four checks. | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-8 | P3 | M2-04 logos | **On first enable, no logo appears at all until the WHOLE fetch finishes -- about twenty minutes on a 1,436-logo list.** `logoHave` is the set a row must be in before it draws an Image, and `Service.qml` populates it only in `logoFetchProc.onExited`. So for the length of the fetch the user has switched a feature on, accepted a 63-host disclosure, and sees exactly nothing change; then every logo appears at once. Observed while taking preview.png on the live install: 355 files on disk, 10 of the 10 channels visible in the guide had theirs cached, and the rows were still blank. The `have` gate is not the wrong design -- it exists because pointing a QML Image at a missing file makes Qt log "Cannot open" per row per scroll (D-LOGO-... the journal-spam finding) -- but gating on a set that only arrives at the END is. Candidate fixes, none costed yet: have the helper emit names incrementally and read stdout as it streams; or re-read the directory on a timer while the fetch runs; or let the row attempt the file while a fetch is IN PROGRESS and accept the log noise only for that window. Fixed 2026-09-24 by the first candidate: the helper emits `{"kind":"logo","name":...}` per file written or found cached, `Model.logoStreamName` parses a line, and `Service.qml` reads stdout with a `SplitParser` batching into `logoHave` behind a 250 ms timer. Counter-proved against a worktree at pre-fix HEAD with the same fixture (20 cacheable logos plus 5 on an unroutable host so the fetch stays alive): pre-fix `logoCount` 0 at +5/+10/+15/+20s, with the fix 20 at every sample. Two of seven mutations survived and both were test gaps, now red. | fixed |
| D-LOGO-9 | P2 | M2-04 logos | **Turning logos off reclaims nothing, and there is no supported way to reclaim it.** The setting stops the fetch and blanks the rows (D-LOGO-3) and it now stays off (D-LOGO-2), but every byte already written stays forever. Measured on the live install 2026-09-24 right after switching logos off: 1,173 files, 40.9 MB, which is 99 percent of that source's whole cache against 468 KB of `channels.json`. The documented ceiling is `MAX_LOGO_FILES` 5000 x `MAX_LOGO_BYTES` 256 KB = **1.28 GB per source**. Nothing collects it: `clear_source_dir` is reached only from `cache remove` and `cache prune`, and prune calls it solely for a key NOT in the keep set, so a source the user still has can never lose its logos whatever `showLogos` says. There is also no eviction for logos whose channel has left the playlist -- a cached file is skipped by a stat and kept. The only reclamation is deleting the whole source, which takes the playlist cache with it. The EPG twin of exactly this was filed as D-GS-4 and produced `cache epg-clear --key K`, a per-feature clear that keeps the source; logos never got the equivalent. Two things make this more than housekeeping. Filenames are `fnv1a32` of the logo URL, so what survives re-identifies the channels the user just withdrew consent for -- the same privacy shape as D-LOGO-4, which was P2 and was fixed only for the source-removal path. And README.md, which ships, tells the user "Turning it back off is one keypress and no dialog, and it stops a fetch already in progress", enumerating what reversing consent does and stopping at the network. `docs/RULING-LOGOS.md` anticipated this in prose after D-LOGO-4 -- "not only who is contacted but what survives the user changing their mind" -- and never gave it an id, so nothing enforced it. The M2-04 acceptance checklist looks at the disk only on the ON side; its OFF line stops at "zero logo requests". Not fixed; filed with the measurement. Costed at roughly 30 lines: a `cache logos-clear --key K` beside `cache_epg_clear`, reusing `remove_cache_subdir` and inheriting `source_dir`'s key guard, called from the existing `queueCacheJob` with no new plumbing. **Fixed 2026-09-24: `cache logos-clear --key K` ships**, the twin of `cache epg-clear`, deleting only `logos/` and leaving the channel list, the guide data and the directory alone. Idempotent, key-guarded through `source_dir`, and the payload carries counts rather than names because a logo filename is the fnv1a32 of its URL and would re-identify what the user just withdrew. Ten new tests, all ten mutations killed. README amended: the sentence that stopped at the network now says the pictures stay and how to reclaim them. Still OPEN as a product decision: whether turning logos OFF should clear automatically, offer, or stay manual -- the verb is the mechanism, not the policy, and wiring it to the OFF edge is a one-line `queueCacheJob` call once that is settled. | fixed (verb); policy open |
| D-LOGO-10 | P2 | M2-04 logos | **A symlinked `logos/` was followed, so clearing a cache deleted files outside it.** Found while building D-LOGO-9's verb, by testing the guarantee `remove_cache_subdir`'s own docstring makes. `source_dir` has refused a symlinked KEY directory since the beginning; the guard never reached one level down to the subdirectory, and `os.listdir` follows a link. So every caller cleared the contents of whatever the link pointed at: `cache remove` and `cache prune` since D-LOGO-4 introduced `CACHE_SUBDIRS` and this function, and `cache logos-clear` the moment it was written against it. Reproduced both ways with a victim directory outside the cache -- two files deleted through the link by `logos-clear`, one by `remove` -- and the payload said `removed: false` while doing it, so even the report was wrong. Not remote and not cross-user: the cache is 0700 and a link has to be placed there by the user or something running as them, and a user who symlinks the directory to another disk to save space is the realistic case. Fixed in the shared primitive rather than the new caller, so `remove` and `prune` are covered too: `remove_cache_subdir` returns False for a link and `subdir_census` measures nothing through one, since counting a target that cannot be freed would report space that was never reclaimed. A link is reported as removing nothing rather than raising, because the caller is a queued background job and leaving a thing we do not own alone is the safe answer. Two tests, one per verb, red against the pre-fix helper and green against the fix. Also corrected the docstring that claimed a symlink INSIDE was left: it is unlinked, never followed, as `remove_regular` always said. **Second round, 2026-09-25**: the adversarial review reproduced the defect THROUGH that first fix. `if os.path.islink(path): return False` is a check on a NAME followed by an operation on the same NAME, and a swap landing between them deleted two files outside the cache while the payload still said `removed: false`. Measured both windows: a swap in the census window made it report 16 bytes freed that were never touched, a swap in the removal window emptied the victim directory. Fixed structurally rather than by narrowing: `O_DIRECTORY \| O_NOFOLLOW` refuses a link AT OPEN and yields a handle on the inode that was checked, every lstat and unlink is `dir_fd`-relative to it, and the rmdir is relative to a handle on the parent. Nothing re-walks a path, so there is no window. Two more tests -- one asserting no name-based link check is consulted at all, one injecting the swap between the open and the unlinks -- and 10 of 11 mutations killed. The survivor is `rmdir(name, dir_fd=parent)` reverted to `rmdir(path)`: it resolves the same name in the same parent, so it differs only if a parent COMPONENT is swapped, and rmdir removes only empty directories. Kept anchored for consistency, recorded as undistinguished by any test. | verified fixed |
| D-REL-3 | P2 | release | **Shipped prose tells the user to run `omarchy-iptv <subcommand>`, and `omarchy-iptv` is on nobody's PATH.** The helper lives in the plugin directory; nothing installs a wrapper; the plugin itself resolves it absolutely. So a bare invocation in README.md or CHANGELOG.md, both on the release allowlist, is an instruction that exits 127 on every install. Found 2026-09-14 by the player QA as PLY-WEAK-05 (`docs/QA-PLAYER.md`), which verified `command -v omarchy-iptv` exits 1 and graded README bullet 6's `omarchy-iptv player stop` a P2 documentation defect. That instance was corrected the same day (21487f7 added it, 8f9447e replaced it with the path form) and never shipped in a release. It was given no id and no row here, so nothing enforced the lesson, and it recurred twice: `omarchy-iptv logos` entered README.md at c1f8a16 on 2026-09-23 and shipped in v0.7.6 through v0.7.10, which is `main` today; and on 2026-09-25 the same author fixed that sentence and wrote the identical bare spelling into CHANGELOG.md in the same change, caught by the pre-commit adversarial review, whose refuters found PLY-WEAK-05 and named the rule-13 failure. Both dev instances fixed in 582d439. Filed 2026-09-25 with the check that makes the lesson a call rather than a name: `scripts/release.py check` now scans README.md and CHANGELOG.md as whole text for `omarchy-iptv <subcommand>` not reached through a path, with the subcommand list read from the helper's own parser so it cannot drift, and reports file:line with the runnable spelling. Proved red against the pre-lint release.py (2 failures, 2 errors of 5), then run over history: catches 21487f7:164, v0.7.6:100 and v0.7.10:101, clean on dev HEAD. `main` still carries the v0.7.10 instance until the next release. Seven sibling findings in `docs/QA-PLAYER.md` section 11 remain prose-only, unfiled here. | fixed; ships with the next release |
| F-PLY-1 | P4 | player | **QA-PLAYER section 11 item 4: README says the player is killed "within about four seconds"; the stop budget is 4.5 s.** Filed 2026-09-25 in the sweep after D-REL-3, eleven days after it was written in prose. The lead chose the finding's own second option -- keep the prose, keep the budget -- in QA-PLAYER section 14 item 11 (396a69a, 2026-09-14). | D-PLY-15 | P3 | player | **`player.lock` was not in the README's `Files it writes`.** QA-PLAYER section 11 item 5 and PLY-SEC-08, true at 363ce9c, where the list named `mpv.sock` and nothing else. Fixed 24 minutes later at 8f9447e (2026-09-14), which replaced the socket line with the runtime directory and "a small lock file used to guarantee only one player exists"; carried by every release from v0.3.0 through v0.7.10. Filed 2026-09-25 because the fix was recorded in section 14 item 11 and never on the PLY-SEC-08 row or the F1 cell, both of which still read "missing" eleven days later, and because a filename grep for `player.lock` finds nothing -- the lock was documented by description, which the plan's own method (a read of the section) accepts and a grep does not. | D-PERF-1 | P2 | harness | **The open-budget instrument has never measured what its own comment promises, since the day it was written.** `openMs` (`scripts/dev-harness/shell.qml`) force-lays-out the channel view before stopping its clock, and the comment above it states that without this \"the delegates -- which is where the logo Images live -- are created after the measurement and the budget measures nothing\". Dated: `windowLoader` landed 2026-09-21 at be7fc3c and the lookup was written 2026-09-23 at 2e486da, AFTER it, so it has never resolved. Fixed: the walk now follows `item` and `contentItem` too, and the depth limit rises from 12 with the extra hops. (2) `layoutView` took the first name it resolved rather than the one PRESENTING, so with the guide in list view it forced `channelWall` -- both views exist at all times and only one is visible. Fixed. (3) `realisedCount` used `hasOwnProperty(\"index\")`, which is false for a QML declared property; it now counts by `channelId`. **What is NOT fixed, and it is the substance.** Even repaired, `openMs` calls `forceLayout` SYNCHRONOUSLY in the same turn as the summon, so the view is not laid out when it looks: at 10,000 channels it reports 4 realised delegates for the list and 1 for the wall. A bounded synchronous poll cannot fix it either -- blocking the event loop is precisely what stops the delegates being created. Measuring an open that includes delegate creation needs an async instrument (start, settle, query), which is a design change, not a patch. **Consequence, stated plainly**: every open-budget figure this project has recorded since 2026-09-23, including M2-04's logo numbers, was taken without the delegates laid out, and rule 7's 150 ms budget has therefore never been verified against the thing it is about. The raw figures at 10,000 channels are 14 ms median and 28 ms max on both views, which is comfortably inside 150 ms -- but that is the summon, not the open. **PO ruling 2026-09-25: this does NOT hold 0.8.0.** The defect is in the instrument, not the product; it predates the channel wall by two days and has affected every figure since, so holding this release would not un-take those measurements nor make the wall's own numbers appear. The release ships with the budget UNVERIFIED and both this row and F-WALL-1 saying so in terms. What the release must not do is quote the 14 ms median as the open budget, and nothing shipped does. Checked rather than asserted, and the first version of this sentence was WRONG: the shipped `CHANGELOG.md` does carry timing figures, at the 0.7.5 entry -- 165 ms to 11 ms per keystroke, 212 ms to 64 ms worst case. Those are SEARCH figures taken with qmltestrunner in the QML JS engine (F-PERF-1's thread), not open figures taken with `openMs`, so this defect does not touch them. No shipped file states an open-budget number, and `docs/UX.md`, which does state the wall's delegate counts and paint times, is not on the allowlist. | open, ships with 0.8.0 by ruling; lookup and view-choice fixed 2026-09-25, the design gap is not |
