| M2-12 | - | dead channels | **Dead channels stop costing you anything: zapping steps past them, and a failed stream hands the guide back.** Two halves of UX.md 1.3, both written at M0 and neither implemented: "a failed stream must not cost more than one gesture to move past" and failure "must not clear the user's place in the list". `Guide.qml open()` rebuilt from `initialScope` every time, so a failed play cost the query, the scope AND the cursor -- with 0 favourites that is All, row 0, empty search, in a 1,462-row list. NOW: the place is remembered at the one moment the guide closes to play, and restored only when THAT channel is the one that failed and within 180 s, so an ordinary reopen still starts fresh. The cursor lands on the row AFTER the dead one. ZAP SKIP: `Model.zapStep` steps past channels already marked dead -- bounded at 20 so a fortnight-old mark cannot make a group unreachable, never empty-handed (an all-dead ring still moves to the neighbour), and it REPORTS what it skipped because silently walking past rows the user can see would be the guide lying about state. ONE LIVE BUG FOUND AND FIXED IN THE PROCESS: `rebuildDisplay()` set the restored cursor correctly and the very next line in `open()` overwrote it with `cursorFor(rows, playingId)` -- which is 0, because nothing is playing, because the play just failed. The whole feature undone one line after it worked; pinned now as a seam check with the ordering asserted. Eight mutations red. VERIFIED LIVE: searched, moved the cursor to Channel 0012, played it, it failed, reopened -- query intact, cursor on Channel 0013. And on a six-channel ring with two marked dead, one zap from Zap 0 landed on Zap 3. | shipped |
| M2-11 | - | pause | **Pause live TV.** `c` in list mode, and a `pause` IPC verb so a global keybinding reaches it with the guide CLOSED -- which is the point, because it is the first action in this product wanted while WATCHING rather than browsing. MEASURED BEFORE BUILT: across 24 channels on 24 distinct providers, 22 played and **21 of 22 paused and resumed correctly**, buffer growing one second per second paused and playback continuing where it stopped. The first sweep reported 5 of 17 and 17/17 seekable and was THROWN AWAY: the IPC reader matched replies to requests by position rather than by request_id, so a `seekable` query could return a leftover `time-pos` (a number, hence truthy). The tell was an internal contradiction against a careful manual reading of False; fixed with request ids and validated against that known-good reading before re-running. REWIND IS OUT and the naming must never imply otherwise: 1 of 22 reported itself seekable and that one was geo-blocked. Pause length measured at **315 s** on a ~3.8 Mbps stream, the point mpv's default 150 MiB buffer stops growing; a fatter stream caps sooner. A paused player reads HEALTHY to the 10 s watchdog (`statusHealthy` judges "running and answering", not "advancing"), so pausing cannot trip a restart or write a false failure mark -- which mattered more than usual now that marks persist. R7 says a bar state is never colour alone, so paused gets its own glyph (U+F0FD1, confirmed in the installed font with fc-query), its own tooltip word, and its own accessible name -- the last found by MUTATION, along with the glyph branch: both had no test and removing either stayed green, which would have left the one user who cannot see the glyph the one user not told. Seven mutations all red. VERIFIED LIVE through the real key path (handleListLetter -> the Model letter table -> the guide action -> the service -> the helper -> the mpv socket): pressing `c` flipped the service to paused, flipped the footer hint to `resume`, and mpv itself reported `pause True`; pressing it again undid all three. Node 1566 -> 1569. | shipped |
| M2-10 | - | dead channels | **The guide remembers which channels did not work, across restarts.** PO asked for it 2026-09-24; R8, R11 and UX ruling 9 all specified session-only and are amended in place. THE STORE IS THE SOURCE CACHE (`sources/<key>/failed.json`, 0600), not `state.json`, and a design review is why: a state-level map would have marked a DIFFERENT provider's working channel, because channel ids are global across sources, and it would have fallen inside D-ID-1's id-rotation blast radius -- whose board row records the failed map as excluded PRECISELY because it was session-only. Per source, that exclusion survives, for a better reason: a mark whose id the current playlist lacks is DROPPED ON LOAD, so an id rotation, a provider reshuffle and a removed channel all self-heal with no migration. The value is an epoch, not the old "HH:MM" display string, formatted at the guide boundary so rowDetail, rowMeta, rowFailedMeta, rowNoticeEmphasis and rowAccessibleName all keep taking the string they always took -- and because a bare truthiness test on an epoch is a silent midnight bug, 0 being falsy where "00:00" was truthy. Shape is a capped array newest-first (300, 14 days), the savedSearches precedent, because a map has no cross-language eviction order. `failed.json` is in CACHE_FILES so a removed source takes its marks, which is D-LOGO-4 applied before the defect rather than after. Nine mutations all red; one shared JSON fixture both suites run. VERIFIED LIVE: marked, restarted the shell, the mark came back, and the row itself reads `UK | SPORTS - Failed 13:38 - Space to retry` -- observed through a new harness field that composes the delegate's own detail line, because proving the data arrived is not proving the row says so (rule 14). Self-healing verified by removing the channel from the playlist and watching the mark prune to empty. Node 1559 -> 1566, python 596 -> 605. | shipped |
| D-SINK-4 | P2 | security / rule 5 | **The stream URL, credentials and all, is published on the session bus for as long as a channel plays.** `mpv-mpris 1.2-2` is installed and `/etc/mpv/scripts/mpris.so` symlinks it into mpv's system autoload directory; the plugin's spawn argv passes neither `--load-scripts=no` nor `--no-config` (grep across Model.js and bin/omarchy-iptv returns zero for both), so every mpv this plugin starts loads it. MEASURED headless against a local fixture with a SYNTHETIC credential, using the plugin's own argv shape: `busctl --user list` shows `org.mpris.MediaPlayer2.mpv`, and `xesam:url` reads back the URL verbatim, userinfo included. Any process on the user's session bus can read it. Note what is NOT leaking, because it shows the shape of the gap: `xesam:title` reads `IPTV`, so `--force-media-title` is doing its job -- the title had a guard, the URL has no equivalent option and nobody looked. DISTINCT from the `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` residual ARCHITECTURE.md section 6 accepts: that is our argv, transient, and readable only by the same user; this is a third-party script publishing on a bus for the whole time a channel plays. `Model.redactUrls` cannot reach it -- nothing the plugin renders is at fault. FIX MEASURED: `--load-scripts=no` takes the mpris name off the bus (verified, 0 names after). COST MEASURED and real: `/usr/share/omarchy/shell/plugins/services/media/MediaModel.js:112` falls back trackTitle\|\|identity\|\|desktopEntry, so Omarchy's own media widget currently shows the playing channel for free because the helper pushes the channel name into `media-title` over IPC; `--load-scripts=no` takes that away. Three options costed in docs/QA-RESULTS.md (always off / a setting defaulting closed / only when the URL carries userinfo). RULED 2026-09-24, delegated to the lead: option A, `--load-scripts=no` always, AND `--load-scripts` added to MPV_RESERVED. Three things decided it. There is no option D: `strings` on mpris.so shows no script-opts at all, it reads `media-title` and `path` and publishes `xesam:url` unconditionally, so keeping the script and not leaking is not available. What is lost is redundant rather than unique -- the plugin's OWN bar widget already shows the playing channel name (BarWidget.qml:46), so the loss is a duplicate label, and a duplicate label is not worth a credential on the bus. And a setting would have been the wrong shape: the logos setting offers a real trade (disclosure you can count, for a feature you can see), this would offer credentials for a second copy of a label you already have, and a switch implies a trade worth weighing. THE RESERVATION IS THE HALF EASY TO MISS: buildMpvArgv ends `argv.concat(asList(p.extraArgs))`, so user mpvArgs land AFTER the base argv and a pasted `--load-scripts=yes` would win -- defaulting alone would have been a fix-shaped thing, which is D-SINK-2's lesson exactly. VERIFIED after the fix on the real bus, argv built from the shipping buildMpvArgv: time-pos 4.4, video-format h264, path still credentialed INTERNALLY as it must be, and mpris names 0 where it was 1. Playback unaffected. Rule 5's sink list in CLAUDE.md gains the bus, with the generalisation that a sink can be opened by software this project did not write and did not choose to run -- the list had only ever been audited over code in this repository. docs/QA-RESULTS.md 2026-09-24 | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-1 | P1 | M2-04 logos | **The https rule held for one hop.** `fetch_logo` checked the scheme of the URL it was handed and then called the bare `urllib.request.urlopen`, whose default opener follows a 302 to `http://` and to `ftp://` -- so an https logo could redirect the fetch into cleartext, to a host the consent dialog never counted, and an ftp target would even pass the content-type gate because urllib synthesizes Content-Type for an FTP response from the filename. `make_opener` has existed for exactly this since S-06 and its own comment says urllib's default happily follows a 302 to ftp://; `fetch_logo` did not use it, having avoided `read_http_source` deliberately to keep Authorization off a logo request and dropped the redirect guard with it. Breaks RULING-LOGOS rule 3 and the consent sentence rule 6. FIXED with `logo_opener`: https on EVERY hop, userinfo refused on every hop, FTP/file/data handlers removed from the opener; cross-host https redirects allowed and that residual stated rather than rounded away. WHY NO TEST SAW IT: every test in test_logos.py monkeypatched `helper.fetch_logo`, so the one function that touches the network was never executed; seven now run against a real http.server on 127.0.0.1. And the first version of THOSE called `logo_opener()` directly, so the mutation restoring the bare urlopen stayed GREEN across all of them -- third time today a check pinned the piece instead of the seam. Repointed, now red. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. docs/QA-RESULTS.md 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-2 | P1 | M2-04 logos | **Turning logos off did not stay off.** Both settings writers compose the bar entry from `barConfig`, which the host publishes ONE WRITE BEHIND, so a write naming only its own keys carried the previous value of every other key back to disk: turn logos off, then edit a source URL, and `persistActive` rebuilt from a barConfig that still said true and wrote `showLogos: true` again -- the setting came back on by itself and the fetch resumed. The same hole existed in the other direction, `setShowLogos` naming showLogos alone and writing the stale URLs back with it. This is engineering rule 9's own territory and the rule was followed for the echo but not for the COMPOSITION. FIXED by `Model.ownedEntryPatch`: every write states all three owned keys at their current effective values, so both writers are idempotent and neither can revert the other. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. docs/QA-RESULTS.md 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-3 | P2 | M2-04 logos | **Turning logos off did not stop the fetch already running.** `onShowLogosChanged` started a fetch on the on-edge and did nothing on the off-edge, and nothing ever signalled `logoFetchProc`; on a 1,445-channel list the switch the user just threw went on contacting third parties for minutes. FIXED: the off-edge signals the process and clears the `have` set in the same turn, so the rows blank at once. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-4 | P2 | M2-04 logos | **A removed source kept its logos forever, and no prune could ever collect them.** `logos/` is the first SUBDIRECTORY this plugin has created under `sources/<key>/` and `clear_source_dir` could not delete one: `remove_regular` returns False for a directory, so `logos` always landed in `kept`, the `if not kept: os.rmdir(path)` never fired, and `cache prune` classified the whole key as kept rather than removed on that run and every later one. Removing a source -- or merely editing its URL, which re-keys the directory -- left up to MAX_LOGO_FILES x MAX_LOGO_BYTES on disk permanently, named by fnv1a32 of the logo URLs, which against a public playlist re-identifies the channels that source carried. `cache_epg_clear`'s own docstring promises that removing a source deletes its whole cache directory; it had become false. FIXED with CACHE_SUBDIRS and `remove_cache_subdir`, deliberately ONE LEVEL DEEP and not rmtree: a nested directory or anything unexpected is left and reported rather than followed, and a symlink is unlinked rather than traversed -- both asserted, including that a symlink target outside the cache survives. Verified live: 98 seeded logo files and the source directory all gone on `cache remove`. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. docs/QA-RESULTS.md 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-5 | P3 | M2-04 logos | **The logo column moved the channel name and nothing else.** `nameText` was re-anchored to the logo; `detailText` and the EPG progress track were not, so a two-line row drew the name indented past the logo with the programme title flush underneath, and the 22 px image overhung `nameText.bottom` by about 3 px onto the detail text. FIXED: all three share one left edge, and the image is bounded by the name line's height. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-6 | P3 | M2-04 logos | **The consent dialog said "No channel in this playlist offers a logo" when every channel offered one.** `logoConsentLines` branched on `hostCount === 0`, but a non-https logo lands in `refused` rather than `hosts`, so a playlist whose logo URLs are all http has withLogo > 0, refused > 0 and hostCount 0 -- and the user was told their provider supplies no logos while the policy was declining all of them. FIXED: two different answers, and the all-refused one says so. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. | verified fixed |
| D-LOGO-7 | P2 | M2-04 logos | **The README told every user the feature did not exist, in the release that shipped it.** README.md is on the allowlist and `omarchy plugin add` is a whole-repo clone, so line 100 -- "Channel logos are not implemented yet" and "nothing displays them yet, there is no reason to run it" -- would have landed in every plugin directory alongside the working feature. The settings table was also missing its `showLogos` row. Nothing in the gate joins the README's prose to the manifest's settings, which is the rule 13 shape again. FIXED: the paragraph is present tense and states the guarantees that are now true, and the table has its row. Found by the 0.7.9 preflight. | verified fixed |
| D-A11Y-8 | P2 | M1-09 guide | **One unguarded service call in 0.7.8 stopped the guide from finishing `open()`, and disabled 28 accessibility assertions for a release.** `Guide.qml:709` added `if (root.serviceReady) root.service.recheckBuild()` for D-HOST-2. `serviceReady` says a service object is attached, not that it has this method, and the file's OWN header two lines in says every service access is guarded so the guide still loads against a service that lacks it -- every other call in the file spells it `typeof root.service.X === "function"`. Against any service without `recheckBuild` the call THROWS and every statement below it never runs. Found starting work on the a11y harness open items: the baseline run reported 31 failures against a recorded 3, with `GAVE UP waiting for marker PROBE_READBACK_FINAL` on every guide scenario and every tree below its own node-count floor -- the harness saying correctly and in its own words that the run was invalid. Reproduced identically at the `release: 0.7.8` commit in a clean worktree, so not a regression from the day's work. The transformed copy names the line: `GuideProbe.qml:714: TypeError: Property 'recheckBuild' ... is not a function`. NOTHING CAUGHT IT because the bus harness is deliberately outside check.sh (PLAN-NEXT decision 9), so a guide that cannot open was a green commit, and because the instruction for reading it was "compare against the baseline in docs/QA-A11Y.md" -- a step a person performs and therefore a step a person skips. FIXED twice: the call is guarded, and the baseline is now a SET graded by the machine (`BASELINE` in check_bus.py, exit 1 naming any new failure OR any recorded one that started passing). Proven by restoring the unguarded call: 37 failures, exit 1, OFF BASELINE; guarded, 3 failures, exit 0, on baseline. Note the fake service is a double that is HARSHER than the real one -- rule 10 only forbids more forgiving -- and it earned its keep by being stricter. docs/QA-RESULTS.md 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| D-A11Y-7 | P4 | accessibility | The a11y probe reports 64 checks with 4 failures against the recorded baseline of 3 (L2-XT-05/06/11); the new failure is L2-SC-02, expecting the scope name 'All, 10,000 channels'. Found 2026-09-21 by the live pass; not yet isolated whether the string builder changed (D-SG-1's header wording moved to 'in All - N matches') or the probe's expectation is stale. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-21 section 6 NOT A PRODUCT DEFECT. It was a regression in our own INSTRUMENT, introduced by be7fc3c (the harness floating-window mode) and found 2026-09-22. The row's two candidate causes were both false: the string builder is correct (Guide.qml group delegate, unchanged since the original implementation, produces 'All, 10,000 channels' byte-for-byte) and the probe's expectation cannot go stale because check_bus.py computes it at run time by shelling node into our own Model.js. The truth was worse than either. A top-level Window sizes its contentItem from its own width and height even when never shown; one created inside a Component by a Loader does not. be7fc3c moved the guide's window behind a Loader, windowContent reparents into that contentItem, so the copy's contentItem was 0x0, cardHeight computed to -10, BOTH ListViews lost their viewport, and the probe was grading a tree with one channel row and zero group-column entries - while reporting 63 of 64 green over it. Measured: node counts fell 34/38/36/35/55/46 to 28/25/25/24/25/33, and the 10,000-channel scenario became the same size as the 3-channel one. Bisected by running the probe at be7fc3c^ and at dev on the same nested display. Fixed by sizing the contentItem in the T5 transform; every node count is restored exactly and the probe is back to its recorded 3-failure baseline, L2-SC-02 gone. No user was ever affected: the group column paints correctly on screen (its pixels were measured on 2026-09-21), and nothing this guide declares reaches assistive technology on this desktop anyway (D-GS-3). | not a defect |
| D-SINK-12 | P2 | sinks | **The same cut-before-redact leak at a second sink, found while repairing the first.** `drain_launch_stderr` redacted each 4096-byte `os.read` on its own, so a URL straddling a read boundary was redacted in halves and the survivors reached `launch_reason`, which is the text of a desktop notification. Swept every boundary offset with the shipping function: the path leaked (`http://host.example/li`) and at other offsets the username. Redaction is not distributive over a split, which is the one-sentence form of this whole defect class. Fixed by redacting whole LINES and carrying an unterminated one, since mpv's stderr is line-oriented and a URL never spans a newline. The first repair still leaked at **33 of 48 offsets** because it FLUSHED an over-long partial line, and a flush is itself a cut; an over-long newline-free line is now dropped, which loses nothing a person would read. Sweep of 64 offsets: 0 leaking. Found by the helper lane while fixing the clean_detail blocker, filed the day it was written | fixed |
| F-PANEL-1 | P3 | guide | **`programmeDetail` composes and redacts a channel name the panel never draws.** Raised by the model lane while closing F-SINK-10: the function returns a `channel` field, pays the redaction cost for it, and the detail panel renders the title, the time range, the fields and the next line -- never the channel. Either the panel should draw it (it is the one thing telling you WHICH channel the description belongs to, and the panel can be opened from a list where the cursor row is not otherwise visible) or the field should go. A UX decision, not a defect in the code; pinned by a check so it cannot drift while it is open **FIXED 2026-10-03.** The panel draws it, under the programme title on the caption rung. The argument that settled it: the panel COVERS the list, so the cursor row -- the only thing on screen naming the channel -- is gone the moment the panel opens, and the title above is the PROGRAMME's name, which on a film channel looks nothing like the channel's. Live: `The Ring Two` over `00s Replay`. The check that pinned the question was written to force it and did; removing the line again turns it red | fixed |
| D-EPG-5 | P2 | epg | **One of the 227 matched pairs is the wrong channel, and the project already owned the normaliser that prevents it.** The precision half of M4-01's acceptance criterion, run at last: 226 of 227 pairs are right and one is not. `Bloomberg TV (1080p)` married the guide's `Bloomberg TV+`, whose own description opens "Bloomberg TV+ is a live 24-hour global business and financial news channel", and landed the programme `Latitude` on it. The cause is that `epg_name_key` folds on `normalize_text`, where `+` becomes a space, while this helper's own `normalize_id_text` KEEPS `+` and `*` with the docstring "because they are the difference between two channels rather than noise inside one". The same fold is why ten channels were dropped as ambiguous: four News12/News12+ pairs and Tennis Channel 2 / Tennis Channel +2, each distinct under the other fold. Measured repair, end to end on the frozen inputs: matched 227 -> 226 (the set difference is exactly the Bloomberg row), nameIndexed 1443 -> 1453, nameDroppedPlaylist 10 -> 0, and no confirmed pair moves **FIXED 2026-10-03**, by using the fold this project already had: `epg_name_key` builds on `normalize_id_text`, not `normalize_text`. End to end on the frozen real inputs, matched 227 -> 226 and **the one pair that left is exactly `BloombergTV.us@US`, with nothing joining in its place**; `nameIndexed` 1,443 -> 1,453 and `nameDroppedPlaylist` 10 -> 0, so the ten `+` rows the fold had denied are back in the index. On the audit fixture the same change removes the wrong pair AND returns News12 Long Island, so the matched count does not move while its membership does -- which is the repair's whole shape in one line. Reverting the one word turns four of the precision checks red | fixed |
| F-EPG-11 | P2 | epg | **The id the audit used as its ORACLE was a stronger matching strategy than the matcher.** A declared guide channel id sits verbatim in the stream URL of 185 of the owner's 1,453 rows and nothing read it; the matcher reached its pairs entirely by name. Measured on the frozen inputs before anything was built: of those 185 rows, 145 were already paired with THAT SAME guide channel by name, 0 with a different one, and 40 with nothing. ROW-WISE the disagreement set is EMPTY, which is the strongest safety evidence available; CHANNEL-WISE the address overrules the name on exactly 1 of the guide's 427 declarations, correctly, and leaves the row that held it with no guide channel -- so "the address never overrules the name, it only fills in" is withdrawn as an overstatement of a row-wise result. 185 is the stream alone, the only field the strategy indexes; 193 is the stream UNION the logo, which is the id ORACLE's denominator and where the 153/0/40 split came from. The mapping is exactly 1:1 both ways: no guide id is claimed by two rows and no row carries two **FIXED 2026-10-04**: `match_xmltv_channel` gains an ADDRESS strategy, ranked below the declared tvg-id and the feed base and above the name, reading an alphanumeric run of at least 12 characters in the row's STREAM url that equals a declared guide id. It names no provider and no id shape, and a non-hex case is in the fixture. matched 226 -> 265 (185 by address, 80 by name), gaining 40 and correcting 1. The logo is deliberately not read: recall cost 0, and 7 of the 8 logo-only rows stream from a different distributor than their artwork | fixed |

So the evidence base of an open product decision is weaker than it reads. Found by the oracle lane; evidence in `docs/QA-EPG-PRECISION.md` 14.1 and 15. It also carried the id F-EPG-11 for an afternoon, which is the collision the integration note in that document records **RE-MEASURED AND ACCEPTED 2026-10-06**: on the live install's 1,450-row source the shape holds -- 106 rows carry a 24-hex run in both fields and the two differ on 48, 45.3 per cent, against 105 and 47 on the frozen 1,453 -- so this is a property of the provider's data, not of one snapshot. The consequence is already in the shipped matcher: the `EPG_MATCH_KEYS` docstring says the logo url is deliberately not indexed, gives this finding's reason (7 of the 8 rows it would add stream from a different distributor than the artwork) and concludes "Artwork provenance is not stream identity". | F-EPG-15 | P2 | epg | **"An unknown bracket marker means silence, not a wrong match" was false, and it was the stated reason for a ruling the product owner had just taken.** Found by the adversarial pass on the ruling itself, which ran the real `epg` verb on constructed inputs rather than reading the claim. Narrowing makes the name key LONGER, and the words inside an unknown span can complete a DIFFERENT guide channel's whole name: `ESPN [Deportes]` keys as `espn deportes` and matches a declared `ESPN Deportes` where the broad pattern keyed `espn` and matched `ESPN`; a separator row `===[ SPORTS ]===` keys as `sports` and matches a channel called `Sports`, where the broad pattern emptied the key and matched nothing by accident. So both patterns have a wrong-match route and they point opposite ways -- the broad one deletes a span that carried identity and lands on the generic channel, the narrow one keeps a span that was an annotation and lands on the specific one **FIXED 2026-10-04**: the justification is corrected in the helper, this board and QA-RESULTS, and `test_an_unknown_span_can_still_land_on_the_wrong_channel` asserts the route rather than leaving it to prose, red against the broad pattern. The ruling stands: on this data the bracket content IS the two markers, and deleting unknown identity is the worse of the two failures. Exposure on the owner's 1,453 rows is zero either way | fixed |

The helper reads `seekable-ranges`, clamps a backward target to the floor plus 2 s and a forward one to the edge minus 0.5 s, decides moved-or-refused from its own read rather than from mpv's `success`, and the footer says "As far back as it goes" with the number -- a clamp is always said, never silent.
