R11 carries the comment "The same pair as R10, for the same reason" and then asserts the stored reading RISES by 4 s within 2.5 over a 3 s sleep -- the exact assertion F-RWD-22 removed. Measured 2026-10-06 on the tree: `FAIL R11 the PLAYER's own reading rises (35.754 -> 35.754)`, one failure in 112 outcomes, no reply having landed in that window (load average 5.28 on four cores, which makes the chance worse but is not the cause). The SHOWN half passed in the same run, as it must -- `behindLive` is computed from the local clock and owes nothing to a reply. So the comment names a behaviour the code does not have, which is the shape rule 13 describes, and the fix is to make R11 the pair it says it is **FIXED 2026-10-06**: R11 asserts never-backwards over its 3 s window and leaves the arrival to R10, which already takes it over a window containing a tick by construction -- paying another 12 s to prove the same thing twice in one run buys nothing. Proven on the data that failed: `R11 the PLAYER's own reading never goes backwards while paused (36.403 -> 36.403)` passes where the old assertion failed on exactly that pair. | fixed |
| F-M3-13 | P2 | process | **The closing round's review: 41 findings across four lanes, 1 P1 and 10 P2, and the P1 was in a lane that had already passed its own gate.** The focus guard read `qs`'s stdout and stderr together and fails closed, so a single diagnostic line would have turned every `key` and `type` into exit 3 -- invisibly, because all eleven call sites drop the status, so the scenarios would simply have scored lower with nothing on screen. It was never observed because the lane could not start a harness and the gate had no contact with the verb. Two further P2s were gate-level: `tee -a` where `-p` is the load-bearing flag, so a scenario piped into `head` truncated the transcript the function exists to protect (3 of 22 lines, 3 of 3 runs), and a mode control hardcoding 644, which is `0666 & ~umask`, so the gate reddened under any umask but 0022. The other seven P2s were SENTENCES: a shipped comment claiming three independent artifacts where two are one field read twice, a count credited to "calling the shipping regex" that no shipping regex produces, a scope banner falsified inside its own range, an instruction to the lead that compares a file with itself, a review finding that was itself wrong about a correct commit reference, a mark quoting a pre-fix measurement nobody took, and a claimed house convention whose own stated grep returns zero. **F-M3-8 holds for the eleventh round**, and the round adds a sharper version of it: the three findings that could have reached a user were all in places no check could see | fixed, and the practice recorded |
| F-HARNESS-5 | P2 | harness | **The focus guard refuses and nothing hears it.** All eleven `"$RUN" key` call sites are written `>/dev/null 2>&1; sleep 0.5`: stderr discarded and the exit status dropped on the semicolon. So an exit 3 is SILENT at every one of them -- the scenario carries on and a later assertion fails for a reason nobody can see, which is exactly the invisible failure F-M3-1 exists to close. Half (a) makes the refusal exist; this is what makes it heard. The transcript does not help here, because the redirection is at the call site and not at the scenario's stdout. Raised by the lane that built the guard, against its own work **FIXED 2026-10-06**: `qa_key` in `scripts/qa-lib.sh` surfaces a refusal as a FAIL line in the shape every scenario's own helper prints, still dropping the keystroke's own stdout, and COUNTS it -- because a FAIL line no summary reads is the same defect one layer along -- so all three callers assert the count is zero. Eleven call sites migrated. Observed on all three scenarios: m3 38 passed / 0 failed, text 7 / 0, rewind 114 / 0, each printing `no keystroke was refused by the focus guard`. | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-6 | P3 | harness | **Two verbs with no caller: `type` and `--to-compositor`.** `run.sh type` is the verb that PROVES a keystroke arrived, which is what F-HARNESS-1 was for, and no scenario calls it -- the only invocation in the repo is `run.sh key` inside `type` itself, so the guard on it is exercised only by the gate's decision table and by a human at the prompt. `--to-compositor` has zero callers by construction: it was built because a future compositor chord is a real case, and an escape hatch nothing uses is one nothing has tested in anger. Decide: a scenario adopts `type` for its search-mode typing, or the board records that both are prompt-and-gate-only. Also corrected here, because the next lane would inherit it: the caller list in the lane brief was wrong -- `key` and `type` appear in exactly three scenario files (m3 eight, text two, rewind one), not in argv, chno, chno-entry, id-rotate, pip, player or sources, where one comment mentions the verb and no line calls it | open |
| F-HARNESS-7 | P3 | harness | **R18 certified a transcript its own cleanup had deleted.** The `run.out` the privacy check swept lived in a `mktemp` directory the scenario's own `cleanup()` `rm -rf`s at exit, so the only thing outliving a run was the summary line -- F-M3-1's failure inside the one scenario that had already solved half of it. Fixed the same day: the transcript lives under `$SCRATCH/transcripts/`, which `run.sh clean` does not remove, and a mutation proves the check red | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-8 | P2 | harness, privacy | **A scenario sweeping its own transcript read a file the `tee` had not finished writing.** Measured 2026-10-06: a line printed and grepped straight back was absent 6 of 30 times with the cores oversubscribed twice over, and 30 of 30 for a bash builtin read. R18 is exactly that shape, so a credentialed URL printed just before the sweep could go unseen by the privacy check that exists to catch it, which is rule 14's shape in a privacy guard. Fixed the same day by `qa_transcript_sync`, a bounded barrier that reports giving up, with R18 gated on it | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-9 | P3 | harness, privacy | **A transcript left to `tee` lands 0644, world-readable.** Measured. A scenario's stdout can carry a credentialed playlist URL and `argv-scenario.sh` drives a synthetic one on purpose, so rule 5 and rule 6 make 0600 in a 0700 directory the only acceptable landing place. Fixed the same day: created under `umask 077` and chmod 0600, with the function REFUSING a directory group or other can enter rather than writing into it, driven against `/tmp` for real. At integration the assertion was made umask-INDEPENDENT, which is the property that actually holds, after the first version reddened the gate under `umask 077` and `002` | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-10 | P3 | harness | **R16's seek-occupancy floors are weak relative to the window they now sit in.** `bseek >= 100` and `brun >= 100` were about 31 per cent of a 32 s window at 100 ms sampling; against F-RWD-25's 62 s window they are about 16 per cent. F-RWD-25's close condition asks for 100 samples per TICK window and the scenario asserts a window TOTAL, which is a different claim. Raised rather than changed by the lane that widened the window, because calibrating a floor needs a real run and that lane held no display. Decide after reading `seek samples` off a green run **FIXED 2026-10-06**: the floor is proportional now, derived from `BUSY_MS / HEALTH_TICK_MS` rather than written as a literal, so widening the window cannot silently weaken it. Observed over two runs at the 62 s window: `at least 2 of the 6 health ticks landed on a running seek` passed with 0 status runs and then with 1. | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-11 | P4 | harness | **`healthLog`'s `marks` saturates at 80 entries, so over a 62 s window the printed list is a prefix of the window.** A diagnostic, not a check: every R16 assertion reads a tally and the tallies are uncapped. Recorded with a comment rather than changed | accepted |
| F-HARNESS-12 | P3 | harness | **`run.sh shot` has no focus guard, and a screenshot of the wrong output is as invisible as a keystroke into the wrong surface.** `shot` grades PIXELS, so a capture of a surface that is not the guide's is graded as if it were -- the same class of failure F-M3-1 closed for keystrokes, on the one verb whose output a human reads as proof. Raised out of scope by the lane that built the keystroke guard | open |
| F-EPG-25 | P2 | epg | **The audit's oracle-independence premise was stated in the present tense and has been false since F-EPG-11.** "The matcher reads neither" was the basis of the entire A/B/C/D grading in Part I, and the matcher now reads the stream-address id for 185 of 264 live matches, so that oracle is circular on every address-matched pair. Fixed the same day under F-EPG-13's banner because it sat in that lane's file and was a present-tense falsehood, but filed here because F-EPG-13 is a row about NUMBERS and this is a row about a METHOD, and it was on nobody's list | fixed |
| F-EPG-26 | P2 | epg | **The frozen inputs are gone, so every frozen figure in the precision audit is permanently unverifiable, and the reproduction recipe cannot be run by anyone.** The 1,453-row `channels.json` and the 427-declaration `pluto-us.xml.gz` lived in a session scratch directory on a RAM disk and no longer exist, which takes 265, 226, 227, the 110 confirmations, the 0.0038 false-confirmation rate, the calibration tables and the 0.72 s grade with them. **RULED 2026-10-06**: they are NOT re-frozen into the repository -- the rows carry provider URLs with credentials, rule 5 forbids that sink and `main` ships what the repository holds -- and they are not written into the user's home either. So the document says so: the frozen figures are dated measurements that cannot be re-derived, the recipe states that it needs a re-freeze from a live fetch before it will run, and every NEW census is taken against the live cache with its date and row count named, which is F-EPG-24's prescription. The cost of the ruling is stated rather than hidden: nobody can ever grade those numbers again | accepted |
| F-M3-14 | P3 | process | **A task brief handed a lane a path to measure against, and the path did not exist.** The brief for the F-EPG-13 sweep said "DO NOT re-fetch anything from the network. The frozen inputs live at <scratch>/m4-accept/" and that directory had already been reclaimed, which leaves a lane choosing between a forbidden re-fetch and restating numbers it did not measure -- rule 14's shape, manufactured by the brief. The lane chose correctly and said so, which is how this was found. The rule: a brief that names an input names the command that proves the input is there, and the lead runs it before sending the brief. Written by the lead against his own brief | open |
| F-HARNESS-13 | P3 | docs | **The unmarked-retraction sweep covered one file of seven.** D-DOC-5's fix marked ten retracted measurements in `docs/QA-RESULTS.md`, and the same shape is as likely in `docs/QA.md`, `docs/QA-SOURCES.md`, `docs/QA-REWIND.md`, `docs/QA-A11Y.md`, `docs/QA-EPG-PRECISION.md` and the SPIKE documents, none of which were opened. Nothing can go red for it: the join between a withdrawal and the claim it withdraws is made by a reader noticing two sections hundreds of lines apart, which is rule 13's failure with no id to hang a checker on. Also here, because it is the same join: the transcript rule reached the harness README and CLAUDE.md but not `docs/QA.md`, which is where a test plan's author looks first | open |
| F-HARNESS-14 | P2 | harness | **A scenario answered "someone else has my port" by killing them.** `rewind-scenario.sh` installs `trap cleanup EXIT` ABOVE its argument refusals, and `cleanup` read the pid holding port 8771 out of `ss -ltnp` unconditionally, so any refusal that had started nothing killed whoever held the port -- worst of all on the `port 8771 is already in use` refusal, which exists to protect exactly that process. The gate's own refusal probe would have done it on every commit, which is why that probe stubs `ss`. Measured with a decoy listener both ways: gone after a `--tree` refusal, alive with the guard, alive after the port-in-use refusal. This is CLAUDE.md's parallel rule 3 at one remove -- the rule says kill by a pid this run recorded, and a pid read out of `ss` at exit is a pattern wearing a pid's clothes. Found 2026-10-06 by the lane verifying something else; fixed the same day by a `SERVER_OWNED` flag set only once our own fixture server is confirmed listening. **The hazard class is unswept**: a cleanup installed above a refusal that reaps by pattern or by port rather than by something the run recorded, in the other nine scenarios **FIXED 2026-10-06** by a `SERVER_OWNED` flag set only once our own fixture server is confirmed listening, measured with a decoy listener both ways. The hazard class in the other nine scenarios is NOT swept, and that is what keeps this row open in spirit: it is recorded here rather than left to be rediscovered. | fixed |
| F-RWD-22 | P3 | harness | **`R10 behindLive counts UP while paused` held its value about one run in five, and the check was asserting chance.** Its sibling R9 turned out to be F-RWD-24 plus an instrument that read two snapshots for one claim. R10 was the instrument alone, and the review found why: the check had been moved from the PLAYER's stored reading to the service's derived count-up without saying so, so it could no longer go red for readings that stop arriving. Split into both, it diagnosed itself on the first run **SETTLED 2026-10-03**: the shown count-up rose 12.375 -> 16.375 while the stored reading held at 12.375, because the stored one is written only when a control reply lands and whether one lands inside any given three seconds is chance -- the health tick is 10 s. The product was never late; the old check asserted that a reply happened to arrive, which this product does not promise. R10 now asserts three things: the shown count-up rises (the user-visible promise), the stored reading never goes backwards, and over a window that contains a tick by construction it rises (11.136 -> 24.357 over 15 s). Five runs of the old check across two days, one red each time, for a claim nobody had meant to make | fixed |
| F-RWD-23 | P3 | harness | **For up to about 200 ms after a control helper exits, `controlKind` still names the run that finished while the slot already reads free.** Measured by the QA lane at 100 ms cadence (QA-REWIND 9.5): 2 samples of 128 saw `controlRunning` false with `controlKind` still set -- the `Process` has exited and its `StdioCollector` is still draining (`waitForEnd: true`), so `running` falls before `handleControlResult` runs and clears the kind. Harmless to everything shipped: `Model.healthBusy` answers FALSE there, which is correct because nothing is running, and `Model.healthTick` then resets the skip counter rather than advancing it. An instrument that keys on the KIND alone mis-reads it, which is why R16's checks key on the slot being HELD. (The lead's first version of this row described the window in the OPPOSITE direction -- running true, kind empty -- and called `healthBusy` true there "the conservative side", which is wrong twice: that is not what was measured, and under `healthTick` a busy tick ADVANCES the counter toward the restart rather than resetting it. The review's instrument and equivalence lenses both caught it) **ACCEPTED 2026-10-06, verified by calling the functions rather than reading them**: `Model.healthBusy({running: false, kind: "seek"})` returns false and `Model.healthTick(2, false)` returns `{check: true, restart: false, skips: 0}`, so in the handover window the counter RESETS and a health check runs -- harmless, and the direction the corrected row states. The finer measurement the row leaves open needs a sampler faster than 100 ms and would settle nothing: R16's checks key on the slot being HELD, which is the lesson. Kept so the next instrument does not key on the kind. | accepted |
| F-RWD-18 | P3 | harness | **R14 cannot go red for a missing D10 health exemption.** Found by the review's tests lens: three 10 s health strikes cannot occur inside a 1.2 s burst of 220 ms helper runs, so the check that "the burst never trips the restart" passes with or without the exemption. The exemption's effect (`healthSkips` stays 0 across a long held key) is observable only over three ticks, 30 s, on a stream deep enough not to hit the floor; the local stream's 30 s window hits it in a few seconds and the service then refuses locally without spawning. So the exemption is marked UNVERIFIED on the plan (rule 14) until a scenario holds alternating `b`/`w` presses for 30 s on a deeper window; `Model.healthTick` itself is node-tested and the one-line predicate the service passes it is QML (F-RWD-19) **FIXED 2026-10-03**: the predicate is `Model.healthBusy` and the service reads it from ONE property the health timer also reads; the harness samples `healthBusy`, `controlRunning` and `controlKind` at 100 ms through a new `healthWatch` verb, and R16 holds a key for 32 s against a tree whose helper sleeps 4 s per seek, so every health tick lands on a running seek by measurement rather than by luck (294-296 of 320 samples with a seek holding the slot, 0 status runs in the window, all three ticks busy, on every run). The red proof is a prepared tree with the exemption removed, run through the new `--tree` flag: the three-strikes restart ACTUALLY FIRED -- `healthSkips` reached 2, the player pid changed and the intent counter moved -- 5 checks red where the same tree with the exemption is 105 of 105. What stays UNVERIFIED and says so in QA-REWIND 9.8, three gaps and not one: the step from "the predicate is right on a 4 s run" to "the restart never fires under a held key on a 220 ms run", which is argued from `Model.healthTick` and R13/R14 rather than observed; the third strike with the exemption IN place over longer than the one 32 s window measured (F-RWD-25); and the handover window's size at finer than the 100 ms sampler (F-RWD-23) | fixed |
| F-RWD-19 | P3 | service | **The seek queue's decisions live in QML and no unit test drives them.** Found by the review's tests lens (rule 12): `applyRewind`'s entry-id drop, the `pendingPlayId` guard, the live-queued reset, `clearRewind` on stop, the paused-since choice, the throttle, the health-busy predicate. The scenario observes their outcomes on a real player (R7, R9, R12, R13, R14) and that is calling the shipping path, not mirroring it, but a reducer a node test could drive would let each decision be pinned by a mutation. Deferred rather than refactored on the day of the cut **FIXED 2026-10-03**: `Model.seekQueueStep` takes the queue's own three fields and an event carrying what the service knows, and returns the plan the service obeys -- the refusals, the coalescing, the spawn gate, the stale-reply drop, the throttle restart; `Model.rewindApply` is what a reply means for the readout on all four reply paths; `Model.healthBusy`, `Model.canRewindNow` and `Model.behindTickRunning` are the three remaining gates. 27 node checks and a QML spec case, all seen red against the shipping tree (1852 checks / 27 failures, spec 70/1), and 28 named mutations each reddening exactly the decision it broke. Three things stay in QML and say why: the fallback for a `runControl` that refuses (unreachable by construction, since the reducer checks the same flag in the same synchronous turn), the `Qt.callLater` ordering that makes a queued zap outrank a coalesced press, and which events count as the player going away | fixed |
| F-UX-4 | P2 | guide | **Behind live, the footer's hint row could not be fitted any more and `j/k move` elided off the left.** Found by the review's UX lens with the shipping functions: the fullest playing row behind live is 19 pairs, 213 characters; `fitFooterHints` could drop its seven droppable pairs and no more, leaving 129 against the widest card's 107, where before M5-01 the irreducible row was 99. The three rewind pairs are the 30 characters. The F-UX-2 fit tests never fitted the widest shipped row, which is why the reintroduction passed green **FIXED 2026-10-03**: `b back`, `w forward` and `g live` join `FOOTER_DROP_ORDER` last -- after everything the previous order gives up, because behind live they are the keys that act -- so the row reaches 99 again; three node checks pin the fullest row, the fit at 107 and the order, and the mutant that keeps the trio undroppable is red | fixed |
| F-UX-5 | P2 | guide | **The footer's state line was the first casualty of a long channel name.** Found by the review's UX lens: composed after the name, `1:32 behind live` sat at the right end of a status text that elides on the right, so on an ordinary `(1080p)`-marked name the number was gone whenever it was non-empty **FIXED 2026-10-03**: the state leads the line (`▶ 1:32 behind live · 48 Hours (1080p) · s stop`), the bar's own rule -- the name is the part that can lose characters -- applied to the footer; design 2.5 amended | fixed |
| F-UX-6 | P3 | guide | **The guide's `b` said "Nothing to rewind yet" for up to a status tick after a zap, while the IPC verbs spawned the helper.** Found by the review's UX lens against design 12.1's "the first press after a zap must spawn the helper": `canRewind` needs a range read, and before any reply has carried one the guide refused locally for up to 10 s **FIXED 2026-10-03**: "not yet rewindable" is a verdict only once a range has been read (`rewindKnown`); before that the key falls through to the service, which spawns the helper to decide, and the reply's transient says what happened | fixed |
| F-M5-1 | P2 | process | **The M5-01 review: 43 findings, 36 confirmed, 4 partial, 3 refuted only as already fixed at HEAD, and both blockers of the integrated tree were things the lead already had data for.** One: `back N` seeked ten seconds whatever N was (F-RWD-17), and the lead's own live pass had recorded it as a landing past the target and filed it under F-RWD-8. Two: the helper's deciding read raced the player (F-RWD-15), found by the scenario's first integrated run and settled by measurement. Also: the lanes' mutation ledgers (J1-J18, P1-P17, ten stub mutations) were cited by count and held nowhere in the tree; the test double was more forgiving than mpv in two more places (the stub moved before replying, the fake delivered log lines to clients that never subscribed); a vacuous `all` over `[]`, a vacuous `[] == []`, and a node count the run cannot print. Evidence: QA-RESULTS, the three review batches and the verdicts section **ALL CLOSED OR FILED 2026-10-03** (0349033 through the cut commit): F-RWD-11, 15, 16, 17, F-UX-3..6, F-SINK-12, F-HARNESS-3 fixed; F-RWD-18, 19 filed open | fixed |
| F-RWD-20 | P4 | docs | **A bare `back` or `forward` over `omarchy-shell` is refused by Quickshell's IPC before it reaches the plugin.** Measured on the owner's shell after the 0.11.0 update: `omarchy-shell io.github.rmcdavid.iptv back` answers "Too few arguments provided (1 required but 0 were provided)" from qs itself; `back ""` reaches the verb and is the 10 s step, `back 30` is 30. The service lane had listed exactly this as unmeasured. The README's `(default 10)` on the verb line read as if the bare form worked **FIXED 2026-10-03**: the README line says the argument is required and that `back ""` is the step; `contrib/bindings.lua` already passes numbers | fixed |
| F-MPV-2 | P3 | test double | **The scenario stub delivered an mpv EVENT only to the client whose command caused it, which is F-MPV-1 again in the third double.** F-MPV-1 was filed and closed in 2026-09 for `STUB_MPV` and `FakeMpv`, and its settled rule is the one real mpv follows: an event broadcasts to every connected IPC client, a command reply is point to point, a log message reaches only the clients that subscribed. `scripts/qa-stub-mpv.py` was never audited against it, and the M5-01 seek followups it gained on 2026-10-03 went to the issuing connection alone -- the lead wrote that up as "unmodelled by design, since the plugin holds one control connection per run", which is wrong on its premise: the detached player keeps a persistent observer connection beside the transient control one, so the real system has two. No shipped defect follows (the service's event router answers `ignored` for every event name it does not know, and the helper's seek wait already records a foreign `seek` event as an accepted residual), but the double could not express a mis-routing bug, which is exactly what made D-PLY-12 inexpressible **FIXED 2026-10-03**: delivery is `followup_targets(event, issuing, conns, log_conns)`, a function the self-test calls (rule 12), the serve loop registers and unregisters connections, and `dispatch` decides only WHAT follows. Three mutations red: targets return the issuing connection alone (the decision), the serve loop writes to the issuing connection alone (the wiring, caught by a two-client socket case), and a closed connection left in the list | fixed |
| F-UX-3 | P4 | bar | **The paused tooltip said paused twice.** `Paused 48 Hours (1080p)` on the first line and `paused · 0:12 behind live · up to 0:39 back` on the second, because the shared state composer carries the word for the footer, where it is needed **FIXED 2026-10-03**: `barTooltip` composes its state line without the word; the footer and the accessible name keep it | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-3 | P3 | harness | **`qa_delta` is integer-only, so the rewind scenario's two count-up checks could never pass.** R10 and R11 subtract two `behindLive` readings, which are floats; `qa_delta` (built for counters) printed NODELTA on both, and the check read it as a failure. Invisible on the pre-feature tree, where the field was NOSTATE anyway, and found on the first integrated run where the count went 11.184 -> 13.215 and 22.427 -> 26.898, both inside the band. The scenario's R3 socket-side edge check had the same shape: `ahead <= 1.0` on a 2 s-segment stream whose cache end moves in 2 s steps **FIXED 2026-10-03**: a float `fdelta` for the two checks; R3's socket read allows a segment plus the margin and prints the value | fixed |
| F-RWD-14 | P3 | harness | **`run.sh ipc` hardcodes the IPC target `harness`, so a scenario that must reach the plugin's own `IpcHandler` carries its own client.** `rewind-scenario.sh` does (its `pipc`), after SPIKE 12.5 found the form that works. A `plugin-ipc` subcommand on `run.sh` would let every scenario share it; until then each new scenario that needs the plugin's verbs will copy the dozen lines **FIXED and VERIFIED END TO END 2026-10-06.** `run.sh plugin-ipc` carries the incantation; the only caller was migrated to it at integration, and `PLUGIN_ID`, whose two readers were the two lines removed, is gone. The lane that built it held no display and marked the reply UNVERIFIED; measured since against a live harness -- `plugin-ipc status` returns the plugin's own JSON (`sourceHost` redacted to `local file`), an empty call exits 2 with its message, no instance exits 255 byte-identically to `ipc`. The shared verb reads `-p $(qs_root)`, so an instance set in the environment can no longer have `ipc` talking to one shell and `pipc` to another inside one run, which the hand-written copy allowed. | fixed |
| D-SRC-11 | P3 | sources | **`state source update --epg-url` is a documented helper command whose effect the running service silently reverts.** Measured on the live install 2026-10-03 while setting a guide URL for the owner: the helper writes the record (verified against an isolated copy of the state directory, where the value persists), and the service's `reconcile` writes the record back FROM the plugin setting on the next start, so the value is gone after a restart with no error anywhere. The plugin setting in `shell.json` is the authority and the record follows it (`activeEpgUrl`, `persistActive`, D-SRC-10); the CLI writes the follower. Setting the plugin entry's `epgUrl` instead worked immediately and propagated back INTO the record, which confirms the direction. Not a data-loss bug -- nothing the user typed is lost, because the user never typed it here -- but a command the help text offers and the running product undoes. Either the CLI should refuse while a shell holds the state, say what it cannot do, or write the setting too **FIXED 2026-10-06**: `state source update` names the fields the shell owns and warns after the write, so a refused update warns about nothing, and the warning carries no URL at all. | fixed |
| F-BOARD-1 | P2 | process | **Five of the nine rows reading `open` were fixed, one of them a day earlier and four of them a week earlier, and nothing could see it.** `scripts/check-defect-ledger.py` makes the board-to-write-up join a call (rule 13) by checking that every id EXISTS in both places. It does not check that they AGREE: a row's state column is still copied by hand, which is the same failure one level up. The proof it is not hypothetical: D-PLY-24's row says in its own text that it is the follow-up to D-PLY-23's repair and has read `fixed` since the 0.9.1 round, while D-PLY-23 read `open` beside it -- two rows that cannot both be true, sitting adjacent for a week. F-SINK-10 read `open` for a day after the commit whose subject line is "Close F-SINK-10 at the sink". All five verified against the code and closed on 2026-10-03. The structural fix is the open question: a state a human copies is a state that will stop being copied, and this project has now learned that twice (F-CHNO-4 was the first) **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-10-03.** The lane implemented all three candidate shapes far enough to measure each against the whole 523-commit history by driving them at every commit, and chose on the numbers rather than on taste. GATE: commit-message reconciliation over rows whose state cell is a bare `open` -- red at 47 of 486 board-bearing commits, five ids ever, four of them genuinely stale and one false alarm lasting four commits, so 0.8 per cent false-alarm commits. It catches four of the five rows of 2026-10-03. REPORT, never a gate: document prose and the noun forms, which reach all five but cost five false alarms because the same construction reads a PLAN's "fixes D-EPG-2" as a report of work not yet started; and the board's own cross-row contradictions, which reach the same four with no git history at all. REJECTED: a state keyword in the write-up, which catches zero retroactively and would begin as 220 markers nobody has written. Measured and rejected outright: dropping the bare-open rule makes the prose shape red at 221 of 523 commits, with one id red at 214 -- the cry-wolf the brief forbade. The escape hatch is the state cell itself: anything other than the bare word exempts the row, which two rows already use | fixed |
| F-M4-1 | P2 | process | **The M4 review: 37 findings, 0 refuted, 23 of them sentences, and both blockers were the lead's.** One: `clean_detail` cut before redacting, so the username half of a credentialed URL could reach the panel and the accessibility bus -- rule 5 broken a fifth time, in the release about rendering provider text. Two: the milestone's headline repair could be reverted whole with the gate staying green, because no fixture pair needed the marker strip in order to match; the test proved the function and not its use. Also: the matcher's guide-side uniqueness is order-dependent (a channel-then-programmes guide lands an arbitrary schedule while reporting it dropped, 2 of 227 rows exposed on the real feed); the acceptance table carried the DISCARDED run's cache sizes, which the verifier proved by rebuilding the helper at the integration commit and reproducing both pairs byte for byte; 125/224 are 126/227; "2 names to 10" counts channels; the promised 232 was never reachable (ceiling 229); and the precision half of the acceptance criterion was never run. Evidence: QA-RESULTS "Review of the M4 round, 2026-10-03" **REPAIRS LANDED 2026-10-03** (193c16d, 1cce59e, 5412d10, 5892b7e): both blockers closed and measured at the sink, the order-dependent uniqueness rule made order-independent, the budget ceilings set from measurement, and the last two open findings (F-PANEL-1, F-UX-2) settled before the cut | fixed |
| D-EPG-3 | P2 | epg | **The detail panel put a fourteen-digit timestamp under the label "Episode" on 4,526 programmes.** `episode_text` preferred `system="onscreen"` and then took "anything else as the provider wrote it". On the project's own XMLTV asset `original-air-date` outnumbers `onscreen` 8,799 to 4,273 AND comes first in document order, so a programme without an onscreen number rendered `20080101000000 +0000`; the same feed's `pluto` system is a 24-character hex id. Only `onscreen` (as written), a bare `episode-num` (the common shorthand for onscreen) and `xmltv_ns` (translated) are episode numbers; every other system is an identifier or a date and is now ignored. A field the viewer cannot read is worse than none, because the panel spends a line on it. **Found by opening the panel on real data in the live pass, not by a fixture** -- every fixture declared a well-formed onscreen number. `EpisodeSystemTest`, 5 cases, 3 red against the old rule | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-2 | P2 | harness | **The one instrument built to prove a row says something could not see guide data at all, and had hard-coded it blank.** `scripts/dev-harness/shell.qml`'s `cursorDetail` composes the row the way the delegate does -- and passed `nowTitle: "", nextTitle: ""`, with a comment above it saying it exists so a scenario can prove the ROW says something rather than only that the service received it. So a blank row read identically whether the guide data was missing, unmatched, or merely never looked up: rule 14's shape inside the instrument. This is a large part of why D-EPG-2 went unseen for the life of the project and sixteen days after it was written down in prose. It now makes the same `Model.epgFields(epgMap[row.tvgId], nowSec)` lookup the delegate makes, and the live pass immediately read `Movies / Now: The Ruins / Next: The Ring` where every previous run had read `Movies`. The two M4 panels gained snapshot fields for the same reason | fixed |
| F-SINK-10 | P3 | sinks | **The LIST's programme title and channel name are not redacted, where the detail panel's are.** Raised by the model lane while writing `programmeDetail`: `epgFields` copies `cur.title` raw (`Model.js`), and `rowDetail` / `rowMeta` / `rowAccessibleName` render it, the last onto the accessibility bus, which engineering rule 5 names explicitly. A URL in an XMLTV `<desc>` is redacted on the panel and a URL in a programme TITLE is not redacted on the row. Nothing in the data here carries one, which is why it reads as theoretical; the argument against leaving it is that the same string reaches two sinks under two rules. Widening the panel's redaction was deliberately NOT done as a substitute **FIXED 2026-10-03** by the model lane, at the sink rather than at one caller: `epgFields` redacts the programme title and `rowAccessibleName` redacts the channel name, so the row text and the accessibility bus carry what the detail panel already carried. Verified by calling them: a title holding `http://u5er:5ecretpw@tracker.example/x?t=1` reaches the row as `Now: Watch at tracker.example`. The row was left reading `open` for a day after the commit that closed it, which is the staleness F-BOARD-1 is about | fixed |
| F-SINK-11 | P3 | sinks | **`redactUrls` is quadratic in a run of scheme-legal characters, at every sink.** Raised by the model lane: the pattern `[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*://...` consumes the whole run from each start position and backtracks out; measured 41.9 ms for 5,000 characters with no space, about 670 ms extrapolated at 20,000. `epgDetailText` added a cheap exit for the common case, which is not a fix: one enormous token that also contains `://` still pays it, and every other sink calls the same function. A guide description, a channel name and an mpv stderr line are all provider-controlled and all unbounded until a cap is applied **FIXED 2026-10-06, and independently differentialled at integration.** Both redactors anchor on "://" and walk outward instead of re-reading the scheme run. Measured by the lead on 21 adversarial credential shapes against the shipped implementation: 0 differences, 0 credential leaks, and the pathological vector `"aaaa."*8000` falls from 2776.110 ms to 0.015 ms. One already-linear vector is SLOWER in absolute terms and the code says so. | fixed |
| F-UX-2 | P3 | guide | **The footer hint row is past its structural limit on the widest card the guide can draw, measured rather than estimated.** Raised by the guide lane during M4-04 with offscreen font metrics (monospace bold, 10 px, 6.0 px per character): the list-mode hint row does not merely sit at fifteen segments, it overflows and elides from the left on the widest card, so the segments a user can read are the LAST ones. M4-04 adds `? help` at the end, which is the readable end, and the `?` overlay is the answer to the row being unreadable -- but the row itself is still the thing a user sees first and it is still wrong. The interaction document already admits the row "has drifted four times". Needs a design decision, not a patch **FIXED 2026-10-03.** The row is now dropped to what the card can show instead of left to elide, because the element elides from the LEFT and so lost `j/k move` first -- the one pair a newcomer needs. `Model.fitFooterHints` owns which pairs go, by a declared order written where it can be argued with: a trip to another screen before a view toggle, a view toggle before a maintenance key, and anything that only means something while a channel plays before anything that acts on the list in front of you. `move` and `help` are not on that list, which is the whole protection for them. The guide owns only the budget and takes it from the row's own font rather than a constant, so a wider font drops one more pair instead of overflowing again. Live on the real card: **165 characters to 95**, eight segments, `j/k move` first and `? help` last. **It could not have been fixed before M4-04**: the row was the only place in the product where the keys were written down, and `?` is what made it safe to shorten | fixed |
| F-GATE-2 | P3 | process | **`.gitignore` does not exclude a virtualenv or a vendored dependency tree, and the byte scans now read the working tree.** F-GATE-1's fix is right -- an unignored file in the tree is a file somebody is about to commit -- but it makes the ignore file load-bearing for the ASCII check in a way it was not before. Measured by the lane that widened the scan: 496 of 1,591 installed third-party python files on this machine carry non-ASCII bytes, against 0 of a 4,000-file sample of the standard library, so the hazard is dependencies rather than the interpreter. A contributor running `python -m venv .venv` in the checkout would have turned the gate red on dozens of files nobody wrote **FIXED 2026-10-06**: `.venv/`, `venv/`, `build/`, `dist/` and `*.egg-info/` are ignored, with the reason written beside them. Demonstrated both ways on this tree: an ignored `.venv/pkg.py` carrying a non-ASCII byte is not scanned, and an unignored stray with the same byte is caught and told to .gitignore itself | fixed |
| F-GATE-1 | P3 | process | **The gate's ASCII and control-byte checks only scan TRACKED files, so a new file is unchecked until it is staged.** Found by the helper lane on itself: its first commit was red for a literal e-acute in a brand-new test file, visible only after committing. The check reports a file count that reads as coverage ("143 files scanned") while silently excluding everything untracked, which is exactly the population a lane is adding. Either scan untracked files too, or say in the output what the count does not include **FIXED 2026-10-06**: both byte scans read the WORKING TREE (`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`) instead of the index, so a file that exists but has not been staged is checked before it can land. A finding on such a file says so and tells the contributor what to do. 19 tests through a new `--byte-scan` door that calls the shipping scan rather than a copy of it, and six named mutations, the first of which is the defect itself and reddens four cases. Proof it was real, taken live: with the lane's own new test file written and not yet staged, the gate read 163 files and said "1 untracked", where the old gate read 162 and would have read it only after `git add` | fixed |
| F-HARNESS-4 | P3 | harness | **The plugin id is one string in three files and nothing checks two of the three joins.** `manifest.json` carries `id`; `Service.qml` reads it into `pluginId` with the literal as fallback; `scripts/dev-harness/shell.qml` hardcodes the literal and injects it as the harness manifest, so the harness's IpcHandler target comes from shell.qml and NOT from the manifest. `run.sh plugin-ipc` (F-RWD-14) checks its own literal against the manifest and refuses a disagreement, which is one join of three. A rename reaching the manifest and `Service.qml` but not `shell.qml` leaves the harness registering the old target, and the symptom is `qs ipc` reporting no such target rather than anything naming the cause. A check that reads the id out of all three and requires one value would make the join a call (rule 13). Found 2026-10-06 by the lane that built `plugin-ipc`, which could make one of the three joins a call and said so | open |
| D-DOC-5 | P4 | docs | **A withdrawn addendum is still the evidence a reader meets first.** The BET `back 30` sighting was withdrawn on 2026-10-03 when F-RWD-17 explained it as a 10 s step whatever N, and this board says so, but `docs/QA-RESULTS.md` still carries it in the live-pass table (`F-RWD-8 again, 5.6 s past the target, through the plugin path`) and in a bullet reading `evidence added: a second channel ... Still open`, with the retraction forty lines below under a different finding. The fix is a mark at the two sites, NOT a deletion: a withdrawn sighting is part of the record, and erasing a wrong measurement loses the fact that it was made and corrected. Found 2026-10-06 by the triage lane **FIXED 2026-10-06**: both sites are marked in place rather than deleted, and a sweep of the same file found four more unmarked retractions, all now marked. The sweep was bounded to that one file; the same shape in six other documents is F-HARNESS-13. | fixed |
| D-DOC-6 | P4 | M2-04 logos | **Two shipped footer transients are named by no document.** `Channel logos on <SEP> fetching now` and `Channel logos off` (`Guide.qml:294-295`, shown through `showTransient`) are absent from `docs/UX.md` 6.1's transient row and from every other document; a grep for either string across `docs/` returns nothing. The `off` string is the only acknowledgement a user gets for a toggle that has no dialog. `Copied` IS documented; this pair is not. Found 2026-10-06 while closing F-WALL-1, which was the same shape for the wall's own flip transient | open |
| F-EPG-24 | P3 | epg | **Every census number in a shipped comment is pinned to a snapshot that no longer exists, and nothing can grade them.** `bin/omarchy-iptv` states counts over "the 1,453-row source installed on this machine" at roughly a dozen sites, with more in `Guide.qml` and `Model.js`; the live install's source is 1,450 rows as of 2026-10-06 and the matcher reaches 264, not 265. No test can check any of them, because the input is a live provider list -- rule 14's shape in a place nobody had looked. The honest fix is not new numbers but a WORDING one: say in each place that the figure is a dated measurement over a named frozen input, so a reader does not take it as current. F-EPG-13 covers the sites that state a number in the PRESENT tense; this row covers the rest, which are correct as history and misleading as claims. Raised 2026-10-06 by the triage lane, which declined to file it and left the ruling to the lead | open |
| D-EPG-2 | P2 | epg | **The guide-data feature has never rendered a line on any dataset on this machine, and US6 is an M1 must.** Matching is by `tvg-id` only, with a lowercase alias and no name fallback. Re-measured 2026-10-02 against the INSTALLED source (1,453 channels, `tvg-id` coverage 1,453 of 1,453) and the project's own documented XMLTV asset (`https://i.mjh.nz/PlutoTV/us.xml.gz`, 427 channel declarations): the shipped matcher renders **0**; stripping the `@SD` feed suffix renders 0 against THIS guide; a normalised-name match renders **232**. The playlist carries iptv-org ids (`00sReplay.us@SD`) and the guide carries Pluto's own (`673247127d5da5000817b4d6`), so the two never meet by id. Independently, across the 180,681 entries of the published guide index, 582 of our 1,453 channels are covered under their exact `channel@feed` id and **735 under the bare channel id**, so the feed suffix alone costs 153 channels. Ambiguity is small enough to match safely: 3 of 424 guide names and 5 of 1,453 of ours collide after normalising, so "match only when the normalised name is unique on both sides" is available. The finding was written in prose in `docs/ROADMAP-PROPOSED.md` section 5 on 2026-09-16, which said in the same sentence that it was not on the board and should be; it had no id for sixteen days, which is the rule 13 failure the rule was written for. Repair proposed as M4-01 in `docs/PLAN-M4.md` **FIXED 2026-10-03 (M4-01).** Three strategies, tried in order, never overriding each other: the exact id as shipped, the id cut at the first `@`, and the normalised NAME when it is unique on BOTH sides. On the frozen inputs the shipping `epg` verb goes **0 -> 227**, every one by name: this guide and this playlist share no id scheme at all. The uniqueness rule drops ten of our channels and four of the guide's declarations rather than guessing. The 232 in this row's original write-up was a research probe's number and was never reachable; the ceiling on these inputs is 229. Integration found the matcher joining on the SEARCH key, which keeps the `(1080p)` and `[Geo-blocked]` markers 1,155 of 1,453 names carry, so it has its own `epg_name_key` now. Evidence: QA-RESULTS "M4 built and integrated" and "M4 live pass", both 2026-10-03 | fixed |
| D-EPG-1 | P2 | epg | **One "runs forever" programme made epg-now.json unparseable, so every channel of that source silently lost its guide data.** `99991231235959` is a common provider sentinel for a 24/7 stream; it parses to 253402300799, which needs twelve digits, and the EPG record format is fixed-width TEN by contract (the readers slice [0:10], [11:21] and [22:] rather than splitting, because bisect over fixed prefixes buys the 10k-channel budget). `%010d` sets a minimum width and not a maximum, so the title was pushed into the number field; epg-now.json is written by concatenation, so one bad programme broke the whole file. Silent: the helper exited 0 with ok:true and nowCount set, so no banner, no warning, no notification - it read exactly like a provider shipping an empty EPG - and `r` rewrote the same broken file. An ELEVEN-digit stop was quieter and worse: valid JSON with a stop of 1000000000 (September 2001), so the row rendered as having nothing on. Found by the 2026-09-22 defect hunt, demonstrated by running the helper, confirmed by an independent verifier. FIXED by clamping in `encode_record`, the one place records are made. Two tests, proven red against the shipped code. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 | verified fixed |
| D-SINK-1 | P3 | security | **`Model.redactUrls` leaked the password tail when the password contained an un-encoded `@`, and it reached a desktop notification.** The userinfo group was `(?:[^@/\s]*@)?`, which cannot span a second `@`, while the host group accepted one - so `http://user:pa@ss@host/...` redacted to `pa@ss@host` and that string went out through `raiseStreamFailure` to `notifyArgv`. The helper's python implementation was correct, because it hands the match to urllib instead of a regex. Found by the 2026-09-22 hunt and demonstrated end to end through the real socket-event path. FIXED: userinfo now runs to the LAST `@` of the authority (RFC 3986) and the host excludes `@`; either change alone still leaks. The deeper fix is the test: the two implementations were pinned by two DISJOINT hand-written vector lists whose every vector carried exactly one `@`, which CLAUDE.md forbids - one rule implemented twice gets ONE fixture. tests/fixtures/redaction-vectors.json now exists and both suites run it, pinning the property (the host survives, no credential fragment does) rather than the formatting, which differs on purpose. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 | verified fixed |
| D-SINK-2 | P3 | security | **The mpv argument filter reserved an ALIAS and left the real option open, so a pasted `--scripts-append` ran arbitrary Lua inside the player.** mpv's own `--list-options` says `--script` is "alias for --scripts-append"; the reserved set named the alias. `--scripts-append`, `--scripts-add` and `--include` were all accepted by both mirrors. Demonstrated against real mpv 0.41 headless: a Lua script loaded that way read `path` - the credentialed stream URL - and wrote it to its own file, and `--include` pointing at a one-line config containing `log-file=` produced a 20 KB log carrying the stream address three times. The helper already DEFINED the normaliser that would have caught this (`mpv_option_base`) fifteen lines below the filter that ignored it. FIXED: both mirrors match on the BASE option name, so naming a list option once covers every spelling, and `--include` is reserved because a config file can set every other option on the list. `--script-opts` was deliberately NOT reserved - ruling PO-10 keeps it a handoff option that warns - and the suite caught an attempt to, which is recorded in the test. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 | verified fixed |
| D-PLY-12 | P2 | player | **A zap inside the first-load window reports the channel you just left as a failed stream.** Cold start: press Enter on A, then Enter on B before A's picture appears. Real mpv broadcasts to every IPC client, so `player start`'s observer receives `end-file reason="stop"` for its own entry id when the zap replaces it - and `observe_first_load` hands that live mid-stream event to `ended_verdict`, the POST-MORTEM reducer, which reads "stop" as "a load we issued was replaced AND THEN THE PROCESS DIED". The helper replies `{"state":"failed","reason":"Playback stopped unexpectedly"}`. The QML sink at Service.qml:2039-2045 lacks the userStopped/stopping guard its sibling at :2028 has. Demonstrated by measuring real mpv 0.41's broadcast behaviour with two clients and then driving the shipping `player start` against it. Not filed before because BOTH mpv test doubles deliver loadfile events only to the connection that issued them, so the triggering input cannot be expressed in the suite at all (F-MPV-1). docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 FIXED 2026-09-22. `observe_first_load` was consulting `ended_verdict`, the POST-MORTEM reducer, about a LIVE connection. After the fact `reason=stop` can only mean "a load we issued was replaced AND THEN the process died", so the reducer calls it a failure; on a connection that is still open it cannot mean that - it means another IPC client replaced our entry, which is a zap. The live interpretation is now separated from the post-mortem one at that single site, with the reasoning recorded there. Test proven red against the shipped code. | verified fixed |
| D-PLY-13 | P3 | player | **A redirect ended the first-load watch before the real failure could happen.** mpv emits `end-file reason=redirect` while it resolves an .m3u8 master, which is the commonest shape in IPTV. `ended_verdict` already classifies that as non-terminal (kind `ignored`), but `observe_first_load` returned on ANY end-file for its own entry, so the watch stopped before the RESOLVED stream could fail - and an expired subscription failing at exactly that moment is the failure this watch exists to catch. Found by the 2026-09-22 hunt, sibling of D-PLY-12 at the same four lines and fixed in the same change: a non-terminal verdict now keeps draining instead of returning. Test proven red against the shipped code, asserting that a 401 arriving after a redirect is still reported. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 | verified fixed |
| F-MPV-1 | P3 | test double | **Both mpv test doubles are more forgiving than real mpv, which is CLAUDE.md rule 10 exactly.** Real mpv broadcasts events to every connected IPC client; `STUB_MPV` (tests/test_player.py) and `FakeMpv` (tests/test_mpv.py) each deliver a loadfile's events only to the connection that issued it. So any defect that depends on one client hearing an event raised by ANOTHER is invisible to all 531 python checks - which is why D-PLY-12 went unfound. Measured against real mpv 0.41 headless with two AF_UNIX clients and no network. Fix: make both doubles broadcast, then D-PLY-12's input becomes expressible and its fix testable. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 FIXED 2026-09-22, and NARROWER than filed: only STUB_MPV was wrong. FakeMpv's `inject` already broadcast to every connection and its `loadfile` emits no events at all, so it was never mis-delivering - the hunter's "both doubles" was inaccurate and the row said so before it was checked. STUB_MPV now (a) broadcasts events to every client while keeping command REPLIES point-to-point, as real mpv does, and (b) emits `end-file reason=stop` for the previous entry on a `loadfile ... replace`, which it did not before. Both halves were needed: without the second, a zap looked like nothing had ended and D-PLY-12's input still could not be written down. A test asserts a client hears an event it did not cause, proven red against the old stub. | verified fixed |
| D-ID-4 | P3 | sources | **Switching to a second source from the same provider RELOCATES a favourite made on the first.** Two records from one provider whose stream URLs are identical - the common case being a full playlist and a filtered one - produce the same name-keyed channel ids, so `adoptIdScheme` moves the saved reference onto the newly active source's id. Nothing is deleted (the hunter's title said "permanently deletes" and its verifier corrected that): the favourite still shows while the other source is active, but it has moved, and switching back does not move it home. Distinct from D-ID-2. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 REPRODUCED with the shipping functions 2026-09-22 and PINNED by three checks, not fixed - the fix is a state schema change and it is the owner's call. The exact sequence: star on the big list (favourites hold u:4bc351f3), open the filtered list (remap moves 1, favourites hold n:ceb9a086), go back to the big list (Favorites is EMPTY), re-apply the big list (its own remap moves 0, so it never comes back). Cause: `u:` is fnv1a32 of the STREAM URL so two lists from one provider share that space exactly, while whether a row is keyed `u:` or `n:` depends on whether its NAME is unique IN THAT LIST - so one channel has two ids, and favourites are global (D14). channelIdRemap's idempotence argument is sound within one list and silent about two. WHY THE OBVIOUS FIX IS WRONG, and this is the part worth keeping: matching a favourite against any of a channel's alias ids does NOT work, because once the remap has rewritten the favourite to the name key the URL that told the HD/SD twins apart is gone from state - a tolerant matcher then accepts BOTH twins and one star renders as two rows. Demonstrated, and pinned as a check. So it cannot be fixed by changing how we match; the state has to stop discarding what distinguishes them. Also note the same move is made by the helper (remap_state_file), not only by Service.adoptIdScheme, so any fix lands in both - D-ID-3's whole point was that those two agree, and they still do, on this. Not a migration-window bug: the remap is recomputed on every apply and stays non-empty for the life of any list with unique-named rows. Nothing is DELETED - the favourite is relocated and still shows on the other source - which is the correction its verifier made to the lane that found it. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 ACCEPTED 2026-09-22 by PO ruling, to be fixed when channel identity is next opened deliberately. The population at risk is narrower than the hunt implied, MEASURED rather than assumed: the id scheme only reaches `u:`/`n:` when a row's tvg-id is absent or duplicated, and on the maintainer's own 1471-channel list all 1471 tvg-ids are present and distinct, so every row is `t:`-keyed and channelIdRemap is EMPTY - this defect cannot occur there at all. It needs a playlist without unique tvg-ids AND two sources from one provider AND a name whose uniqueness differs between the two lists. Nothing is lost when it does fire; the favourite is relocated and still shows on the other source. THE FIX, when it is made, is to stop storing ONE key: a favourite should carry the channel's name-key AND url-key, matching on the url-key first (which tells HD/SD twins apart) and falling back to the name-key (which survives a credential rotation), because there is no single list-independent key - url-derived ids break on rotation, name-derived ids break on duplicates, and choosing between them by LIST-LOCAL uniqueness is the whole cause. That is a state schema bump with a migration landed in BOTH writers, and D-ID-3's lesson is that the two must agree. Three checks pin the current behaviour, including one proving a tolerant alias matcher would render one star as two rows. README carries a user-facing caveat in the Sources section. | accepted |
| D-SINK-3 | P3 | docs | **Process argv is a credential sink that CLAUDE.md rule 5 does not list, and the README tells the user the opposite.** The composed playlist URL reaches the helper's argv on every fetch and refresh, where any user on the machine can read it from /proc. This is a KNOWINGLY ACCEPTED residual - docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:259-264 names it explicitly - so the finding is not a new leak, and its verifier corrected the hunter for implying one. What is genuinely wrong is the documentation: rule 5's sink list omits argv while instructing the reader to add sinks to it, and the README's own install instructions tell the user to run `omarchy bar set ... playlistUrl "http://user:pass@..."`, which puts the credential in their shell history DURABLY - a worse exposure than the one the architecture accepted. Fix is documentation plus a safer documented way to set a credentialed URL. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, defect hunt 2026-09-22 FIXED 2026-09-22, as documentation, which is what it was. Three changes. CLAUDE.md rule 5 now LISTS argv, with the distinction the rule needed: the helper's transient argv is a knowingly accepted residual reasoned about in ARCHITECTURE section 6, and a transient exposure that was costed is not a licence for a durable one that was not. The README no longer teaches the durable one - its install block used to set a credentialed epgUrl with `omarchy bar set`, writing the whole URL into the user's shell history permanently, while the plugin already ships a first-run form that masks input and reaches neither argv nor the shell; paid providers now go there, and the public no-credential example stays on the command line where it is harmless. The settings section names both escapes plainly, including how to remove a line already in history. ARCHITECTURE section 6 records that /proc/<pid>/cmdline is world-readable where /proc/<pid>/environ is not, so an environment variable would be a real improvement if the transient half is ever revisited - recorded rather than changed, because it touches the helper's interface and that half was the accepted one. | verified fixed |
| D-SINK-9 | P2 | helper | **The new route had two sinks of its own, and the D-SINK-8 prose about it was wrong in the direction that matters.** `/proc/<pid>/environ` is mode 0400, but the kernel's open check is a ptrace-read check that EVERY process running as the same uid passes while the target is dumpable -- so "readable only by your own account", written into the README, CLAUDE.md and ARCHITECTURE by the docs lane and never checked against the kernel, meant every program the user runs. And a crash of a dumpable helper hands its whole environment to `systemd-coredump`, which stores `COREDUMP_ENVIRON` in the journal, durably, readable by the journal groups: measured here, the handler is systemd-coredump, `RLIMIT_CORE` is unlimited, and eight entries on this machine already carry the field. One call closes both: `shield_environment()` sets `PR_SET_DUMPABLE 0` through ctypes (stdlib) before the helper imports anything but `sys`, so a same-uid reader gets EACCES, only root reads it, and the kernel dumps no core. (The first version placed it BELOW seventeen stdlib imports while every sentence about it said "first statement"; the final review measured that the move alone cuts the window by about a quarter and that a split stub could cut it five-fold, so "nothing a child does can shorten it" was false and is gone from every document.) Measured both ways from a same-uid reader: dumpable, readable with the credential present; non-dumpable, refused. The window left is the interpreter starting and compiling the file: 98-113 ms idle at sub-millisecond resolution (median 105 over twelve runs), 264-634 ms with the cores oversubscribed twice over, so the scenario bounds it against an in-run control under the same load rather than an absolute number -- the first bound was an idle-machine 400 ms that would have gone red on a busy box for a reason unrelated to the fix. The scenario re-samples every helper on every 50 ms tick and MEASURES the window rather than assuming it away, after its first draft recorded each process once, always inside that window, and reported the shield absent. Found by the D-SINK-8 review's sinks lens | fixed |
| F-M3-9 | P2 | process | **The D-SINK-8 review: 17 findings, 16 kept, 0 blockers -- and 14 of the 16 were sentences.** Three were the lead's own: the security document of record and the proof's preamble both said a marketplace reviewer "read credentials from /proc" where the record says he reported, from the code, that they could be read (a claim larger than its evidence); and the write-up said 9 of 9 helper tests were red against the old tree when 7 were, the two `--url`-precedence cases passing on both trees by design. | D-TEXT-1 | P1 | guide | **A channel name is a network request: the wall caption renders provider text under Qt's AutoText default, and `<img src=...>` in a name makes the shell fetch it.** Raised by the marketplace maintainer (HANCORE-linux, omacom/omarchy-plugin-marketplace#9628, 2026-10-02 07:34 UTC) against 2d3cee3. StyledText and RichText fetch too (the first measurement's claim that RichText logs the full URL to stderr was re-measured by the fix lane and is NOT a RichText property: the line appears only beside an https img toward a non-routable host). `Text.PlainText` is the only stop: no request in four runs. Escaping is not a closure (AutoText still decodes it); `redact_urls` leaves a fetchable `<img src="http://h">`. `channelWall` has `visible: root.wallView` with a live `model: root.rowCount`, so the first page of captions exists on EVERY guide open, not only on the wall; measured on a replica, to be proven on `Guide.qml` live by the fix. It bypasses the README's own consent gate for third-party fetches (logos off by default, `g` names the host count and waits). Exactly one element: the other 42 name-bearing Texts declare PlainText. Fix: PlainText on `Guide.qml:3228`, an OBSERVED acceptance (harness scenario, probe names, zero requests, red on 2d3cee3), a gate guard that every shipped Text declares its format, rule 5. Evidence: QA-RESULTS "Marketplace finding at 2d3cee3, 2026-10-02" **FIXED 2026-10-02.** `textFormat: Text.PlainText` on the caption (2bd83cb); `scripts/check-text-format.py` in the gate, which found six undeclared Texts, not one -- the caption and five glyph Texts, all now declared -- and the footer's StyledText now under `// MARKUP-EXCEPTION`; `scripts/dev-harness/text-scenario.sh` observes the sink on the shipping Guide.qml through the harness's new `ipc realised <view>` count: against 2d3cee3, 25 requests for the 25 captions the HIDDEN wall holds after the LIST open and 26 after the wall, UA Mozilla/5.0; on the fix 0 and 0, 6/6. What makes it a defect rather than a disagreement is that the SAME architecture paragraph, written 2026-09-22, already names the fix and calls it right: "`/proc/<pid>/cmdline` is world-readable where `/proc/<pid>/environ` is not, so an environment variable would be a real improvement over argv **if this is ever revisited**; it is recorded here rather than changed, because moving it touches the helper's interface and the transient exposure is the one already accepted." It has now been revisited, by an external security reviewer, which is the condition that deferral was waiting for. Three spawn sites carry a URL (`Service.qml` playlist 1871, epg 1888, source probe 3117) and Quickshell's `Process` exposes a writable `environment`, so the change is contained. **FIXED 2026-10-01, product owner's go the same day.** The URL travels in `OMARCHY_IPTV_URL`: `Model.fetchEnvironment(url)` builds the one-variable environment, `Service.qml` assigns it immediately before `running = true` at exactly the three sites, the builders `playlistFetchArgv` / `playlistProbeArgv` / `epgFetchArgv` carry no URL, and the helper's `url_from(args)` reads `--url` if given, else the variable -- `--url` stays for a person at their own shell. Built by three lanes in separate worktrees on disjoint files (helper; model+service; docs) and integrated in one step, because a Service that stops sending `--url` against a helper that still requires it kills every fetch. Quickshell's `Process.environment` was MEASURED before the contract was written: it merges with the inherited environment, re-assignment on the same object replaces the old value, `null` unsets. **Proved on the real `/proc`** by `scripts/dev-harness/argv-scenario.sh`, which adds a source whose playlist and guide URLs carry a per-run synthetic credential, serves them from a server that sleeps so each fetch lives for seconds, and samples every 50 ms. Against the shipped v0.9.1 export: **4 helper command lines carried the credential** (probe, playlist, two EPG runs) and the variable carried it in 0 -- the maintainer's finding, reproduced. Against the fix: **0 command lines, 4 environments by the intended variable**, with four controls unchanged in both runs (a playlist run was seen, an EPG run was seen, nothing left on any command line, nothing in the harness log). The sweep itself was wrong twice before it was right: it carried the credential on its own argv (now its environment), and it matched any helper on the machine and read another lane's concurrent test processes -- running from a worktree with the same example credential in THEIR environment -- as the route working on a tree that had none; it is pinned to the harness's helper by resolved path. Gate 1662 node, 666 python; 13 node checks and 7 of 9 python tests red against 565a982 -- the two `--url`-precedence cases pass on both trees by design, since `--url` already worked, and were proven by mutation instead (the first write-up said 9 of 9, which is the F-M3-8 shape in a number). 100adf9, f8f29d1, 16e1750, 0a1910b | fixed |

| D-TRK-8 | P4 | player | **KNOWINGLY ACCEPTED residual, recorded so it is not mistaken later for an oversight.** While the track picker is open and a HEALTHY player never answers the tracks ask, the recovery edge yields one helper spawn every two health ticks (~20 s) with no termination condition, and the caption alternates "Asking the player..." / "The player did not answer" on that cadence. Reaching it persistently needs `find_player` to miss a process `cmd_status` can still connect to, and the probe path reaps exactly that player. Observed live by the lead: open guide reads `keyboard=true role=catcher`, `type sky` exits 0 and the query reads back `sky`, a real Escape through the guard reaches the guide and closes it, and after a proper close the same key exits 3.
