| D-PLY-14 | P3 | player | **A channel that is playing right now can be shown as "Failed", because the failure mark is never cleared when playback succeeds.** Found by using 0.7.3 on the real display 2026-09-22. Sequence: play a channel, hit a transient error during first load, and the stream then recovers and plays. `raiseStreamFailure` (Service.qml:2367) sets `failedAt[id]`; the ONLY thing that clears it is Service.qml:552, which runs when a play STARTS, not when one succeeds. So the mark outlives the failure. Observed end state: status reported `playing: true` with `nowPlaying` set to the same id that appeared in `failedAt`, mpv reported video-format h264 with 33.9 s of demuxer cache and eof-reached false, the bar widget showed the channel name and live throughput - and the guide row read `Music - Failed 15:07 - Space to retry` with the alert glyph, and carried NO playing glyph and no bold name, because the failure state displaces the playing state. A user watching a channel is told it failed and invited to retry it. THE HOOK for the fix is already there and already runs: Service.qml:1723, where a healthy status check (every 10 s) does `healthFailures = 0` and `checkPlayerChannel(status)` - a player demonstrably playing channel X proves X did not fail, so that is where the mark should be dropped. Model.withoutFailed already exists (Model.js:2275). Now testable: the mpv doubles broadcast since F-MPV-1. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-22 FIXED 2026-09-22. The decision is lifted into `Model.failedAfterHealthy` so a test calls it rather than mirroring it (rule 12), and Service.qml's healthy branch now passes it the verdict `checkPlayerChannel` already computes. It clears ONLY on `agree`, which is the strictest verdict `reconcileVerdict` gives - player up, our source key, and the exact channel the guide names, with the player's own stash saying it is playing - so a channel that genuinely failed keeps its mark, and `unknown` and `diverged` change nothing. Five checks, each proven red by its own mutation: removing the clear (the shipped 0.7.3 behaviour) turns two red, clearing on any verdict turns the strictness check red, clearing the whole map instead of one channel turns two red, and Service.qml no longer calling it turns the rule-14 check red. End-to-end confirmation on a real screen is owed with the 0.7.4 live pass; the fix is not in 0.7.3, which is what is installed. | fixed |
| M2-04 | - | logos | **Channel logos, the guide half, shipped with the setting as the ruling required.** `showLogos` is declared in the manifest, defaults false, and is read by `Model.optInSetting` and NOT `boolSetting`: the existing reader is "anything but false means on", which would let `showLogos: 0` contact sixty-three hosts. A privacy switch whose unknown values mean ON is off by default only for people whose config file happens to be well formed. Consent is `g` on the Sources screen; the footer hint names the DIRECTION (`g logos on` / `g logos off`) so nobody presses it to find out, ON opens a confirm screen carrying the count, OFF is immediate because a dialog in front of the safe direction teaches people to dismiss dialogs, and the button says "Turn on" rather than OK. The row puts the logo after the star and before the name, and a channel without one gets BLANK SPACE and never a drawn placeholder -- the rule the number column already settled. Verified live at both extremes the ruling demands, 27 per cent front-loaded and 98 per cent even: logoColumn true, logoWidth 22, and absent on a list with none. THREE CHANGES TO THE FETCH, each measured first: the extension is gone from the cached file name (Qt 6.11.2 loads a local image by CONTENT, proven with an extension-less PNG/GIF/JPEG and a PNG named .txt, so it gated nothing and an index would have been a second source of truth plus a parse inside the 150 ms open budget); the magic bytes are checked before anything touches disk (with the extension gating nothing, the server's Content-Type was all that stood between an SVG payload and Qt's renderer); and a cached logo is not fetched again -- without that the shell re-downloaded every logo on every start, 1,445 requests to sixty-three third parties per launch, which is the disclosure the ruling exists to bound. TWO DEFECTS FOUND, both live and both fixed: the survey reported `user:pass@provider.test` as a host it would contact, so the consent dialog would have printed the password and promised a fetch fetch_logo refuses -- caught by the shared JSON fixture on its first run, before the screen was drawn; and a missing logo file made Qt log "Cannot open" per row per scroll, so one dead host filled the journal -- the fetch now reports the names on disk (hashes only) and a row draws an Image only for a name in that set, re-measured with a file deliberately missing and the whole list scrolled at 0 warnings. Open budget, seven opens with the list forced to lay out: off 68/16/20/20/14/14/14 ms, on with 10,000 logos cached 26/37/22/21/20/12/12 ms, against 150. Eleven mutations, all red. docs/RULING-LOGOS.md | shipped |
| D-SAVE-2 | P2 | search | **The scope ring read `Favorites 0` on a scope holding three channels.** Two implementations of one question: `channelsForScope` returns the stars PLUS every channel a saved search matches, while `countFavorites` -- which is the number `scopeSurface` publishes -- counted `st.favorites` alone. It predates saved searches and was never revisited when the other half grew a second source, so the label and the rows have disagreed since 0.7.6. The user has no reason to open a scope the ring says is empty. Found on a live pass against 0.7.8, not by the suite: rows were asserted and counts were asserted and NOTHING COMPARED THEM, so each was right about its own question and the pair was wrong. FIXED by making the count the length of the rows -- one call, so they cannot drift again. The new check is the comparison, over five states including a star INSIDE the saved set (so the assertion is not an identity: 3, not 4). Mutation: the old body goes red on four of the five. Live: harness seeded with one saved search, `favorites=0` before and `favorites=3` after, `rows: 3` on both sides. Third divergence in one day closed by making two implementations one call; wherever a COUNT and a LIST are computed separately, the count is a cache of the list with no invalidation. | verified fixed |
| D-SAVE-1 | P2 | search | **0.7.6 shipped rows into Favourites that could not be taken out, and the key that looked like "remove this" did the opposite.** Saved searches put matching channels into Favourites beside the user's starred ones. `x` there called the favourite toggle for every row -- and a saved row is NOT starred, so `x` ADDED a star. Press it again and the star goes and the row STAYS, because the search still matches it. A dead end in both directions, on the one key whose whole meaning is removal. There was also no way to see which searches were saved: Favourites simply filled with channels the user never starred and nothing on screen said why. `Service.forgetSearch` existed and nothing called it. Mine, filed against my own change the day it shipped. FIXED: `x` now branches on WHY the row is there (`Model.favoriteOrigin`). A starred row unstars as before. A saved row forgets the search, and the transient names it and says how many channels went with it. A row that is BOTH loses the star first -- the star is the narrower and more deliberate of the two, and forgetting the search would take other rows with it -- so a second `x` finishes the job; that progression is asserted at 3, 3, 0 rows rather than described. The footer in Favourites now reads `2 saved searches - 9 channels`, below a provider warning and above the plain counts. Six checks, four mutations, and TWO of them survived the first attempt for the same reason: the checks pinned the pieces rather than the wiring. Reverting `x` to the 0.7.6 behaviour stayed green because the check asserted the `favoriteOrigin` LOOKUP was present rather than the branch beneath it, and deleting the footer rung stayed green because the test called the composer directly rather than through `footerStatus`. Both repointed at the seam; both now red. | verified fixed |
| D-STATE-1 | P4 | state | **The two state readers disagree on a numeric `at` written as a decimal string.** `Model.playedRecord` does `Math.trunc(Number(x))`, so `"12.9"` reads as 12. `normalize_played` in the helper does `int(x)`, which RAISES on `"12.9"` and falls through to 0. Same bytes, two answers, on `recents[].at`, `lastPlayed.at` and `session.at`. Found by the shared fixture for saved searches, which included that vector and immediately went red -- the rule about one fixture for one rule in two languages working exactly as intended, on a key it was not written for. Low severity because nothing WRITES a decimal string today: both writers emit an integer, so this only bites a hand-edited file or a future writer that does not. Not changed here, deliberately: `normalize_saved_search` was made to agree with the JS (`int(float(x))`) because a NEW divergence must not ship, but moving the old key is a separate change with its own round-trip proof owed, and this feature had no business touching `recents`. The fix is one line plus a vector in the played-record fixture. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, saved searches 2026-09-23  FIXED 2026-09-23, and it was BROADER than filed. The row said one line plus a vector; it was two functions and five more keys. `non_negative_int` carried the identical `int(x)` and therefore the identical disagreement on every source record's `addedAt`, `lastUsed`, `fetchedAt`, `channelCount` and `groupCount` -- Model.js reads `"12.9"` as 12 and `"1e3"` as 1000 for all five, the helper read 0. A third caller, `normalize_saved_search`, had been given the right form by hand a few hours earlier, which is exactly how a rule ends up implemented three times and correct twice. So the fix is one `coerce_int` with three callers rather than three copies of an idiom: a fourth caller cannot reintroduce the split by writing `int()` out of habit, and a check asserts that `int(float(` appears once in the whole helper and that all three callers route through it. The agreed answer is Model.js's, chosen for two reasons rather than by coin flip: it is the reader the guide runs, so it is what the user's screen already reflects, and it is the more tolerant of the two, so adopting it cannot turn a value that used to be read into one that is not. `tests/fixtures/played-records.json` is the shared fixture both suites run, 14 `at` vectors, 5 record vectors and 7 non-negative vectors, with the divergent cases named as such. Proven red first against the shipped helper -- `AssertionError: 0 != 12 : at='12.9'` -- and three mutations each caught, including restoring `int()`. `Model.playedRecord` is now exported so the fixture can call the rule directly rather than only through `parseState`. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, D-STATE-1 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| F-PERF-1 | P3 | performance | **The search scan costs the whole keystroke budget, where the architecture says it costs a few ms.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` row 4 reasons that "Scanning 10k short strings is a few ms in the QML JS engine; the cost that hurts is `ListModel.append`". Measured 2026-09-23 by calling the shipping `Model.filterChannels` over a deterministic 10,000-channel cache, JIT warmed, 50 iterations per query: `a` **30.124 ms**, `news` 25.019, `alpha news` 23.682, `zz` 23.408, `sky sports` 23.711. The budget for keystroke-to-redraw is **30 ms**, so the worst case spends all of it in the filter before any redraw work happens at all. The cost is the SCAN and not materialization, which is the part the architecture predicted: `sky sports` matches NOTHING and still takes 23.7 ms, while `a` matches 6,877 and takes 30.1. Two honest caveats. This is node, not the QML JS engine -- though V8 is generally the faster of the two, so the shipped figure is likely worse rather than better. And the budget covers more than the filter, so the filter using all of it is the finding rather than a proof the budget is blown; the guide also coalesces at 40 ms, which is what has been absorbing this. NOT a regression: nothing measured today made it slower, and PERF-01 and PERF-02 both pass comfortably on the same build. What is wrong is the architecture's REASONING, which points at `ListModel.append` as the cost that hurts and at the scan as negligible; on this measurement it is the other way round. Re-measure in the QML engine before choosing a remedy -- the obvious ones (a prefix index, an incremental filter that narrows the previous result when the query only grew) are both real work and neither should be started on a node number. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, performance 2026-09-23  MEASURED IN THE QML ENGINE AND FIXED 2026-09-23. The row said not to choose a remedy on a node number, and it was right to: the QML JS engine is about SEVEN TIMES slower than node here, so the defect was far worse than filed. Measured with qmltestrunner on Qt 6.11.2, 10,000 channels, 50 iterations: `a` **212.18 ms**, `news` 159.88, `alpha news` 165.06, `zz` 165.36, `sky sports` 166.54 -- against a 30 ms keystroke budget, so five to seven times over, not marginally. The architecture's "a few ms" was out by roughly fifty times. CAUSE, and it is not the ranker: profiling showed `matchRank` over all 10,000 with precomputed keys costs 2.9 ms, `channelId` 0.6 ms and the bare property reads 0.4 ms. The other 27 ms was one line. `searchKey` has been precomputed since M0 so the guide never folds 10,000 strings per keystroke -- and the ranker needs a SECOND fold, the name alone, to rank a name match above a group match. That one was never precomputed: `nameKey` was present on **0 of 10,000** channels and `filterChannels` fell back to folding it per channel per call. FIXED by emitting it from the helper beside `searchKey`. QML after: `a` 64.44 ms, `news` 19.60, `alpha news` 11.08, `zz` 11.14, `sky sports` 11.10 -- 3.3x to 15x. The two implementations of the fold were already proven identical by the 14 shared vectors in `tests/fixtures/channel-ids.json` that both suites run, so no new agreement risk. The reader KEEPS its fallback, so an old cache still works and only gets slower; a check proves the precomputed path and the fallback produce identical rows and counts, and a mutation that hands the ranker the wrong fold turns six checks red. COST: `channels.json` +12 per cent at 10k (2,613,772 to 2,928,310), helper 572 to 632 ms, and guide open UNCHANGED at 71-74 ms -- measured rather than assumed, since the guide parses that file. RESIDUAL, stated rather than rounded away: the single-character worst case `a` is still 64 ms, over the 30 ms budget, because `total` must be exact for the "First 200 of N" footer and an exact total requires scanning every channel. Absorbed today by the 40 ms coalescing window. Removing it is a PRODUCT decision -- an approximate count -- not an optimisation, so it is not taken here. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, performance 2026-09-23  RESIDUAL SETTLED 2026-09-23, and the remedy this row proposed turns out not to exist. The residual was recorded as `a` costing 64 ms because `total` must be exact for the "First 200 of N" footer, with the fix framed as a PRODUCT decision about an approximate count. Measured rather than assumed: the full scan counting costs 3.53 ms and the same scan not counting costs 3.29 ms, so **the exact total costs 0.24 ms** -- about 7 per cent of the scan and roughly 2 ms in the QML engine. Stopping after 200 matches costs 0.09 ms, so an early exit would indeed be fast, and it would RANK WRONG: filterChannels ranks, a rank-0 match can be the last channel in the list, and the best 200 cannot be known without seeing all 10,000. So the cheap thing is not the count and the expensive thing cannot be skipped while the ranking is correct. Making the count approximate would cost honesty in the footer and buy 7 per cent. NOT TAKEN. The real remedy is a prefix index over `searchKey`, which changes the scan from O(n) to a lookup for the single-character case that is the only one still over budget -- a separate piece of work with its own design, not a product call. What today's `nameKey` fix already bought is the bulk of it: 212 ms to 64 ms on that worst case. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, performance 2026-09-23  INDEX PRICED AND REFUSED 2026-09-23, the second remedy this row proposed that does not survive measurement. After the approximate count was shown to buy 7 per cent, the recorded remedy became a prefix index. `matchRank` matches by SUBSTRING and not by prefix, so the index has to be over character presence, and the best such an index can do is hand the ranker a narrowed candidate set. Measured over the 10,000-channel cache: `a` 8,202 candidates against 6,877 real matches, an **18 per cent** reduction; `news` 87 per cent; `zz` 97; `sky sports` 98. So the index helps exactly the queries that are ALREADY inside budget at 11 to 20 ms, and barely touches the only one that is not: `a` would go from about 64 ms to about 53 ms and stay over 30. The reason is not the index's shape, it is the query's: `a` matches 69 per cent of the list, and nothing narrows a query that broad. NOT BUILT. What would actually remove the case is not filtering on a single character at all -- a one-character search on a 10,000-channel list returns 6,877 rows and is useless to the reader as well as slow -- but that is a UX change with its own surprise (the list stays unfiltered until the second keystroke) and is the product owner's to make, not an optimisation to slip in. Recorded as the costed option. The residual stands at 64 ms on a single character at 10k, absorbed by the 40 ms coalescing window, down from 212 ms before the nameKey fix. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, performance 2026-09-23 RESIDUAL WITHDRAWN 2026-09-23: IT WAS THE FIXTURE. Both measurements built their 10,000 channels BY HAND inside the bench -- name, group, url, searchKey, nameKey -- and the helper writes six fields unconditionally. The missing one was `id`, which is exactly what `Model.channelId` looks for before falling back to hashing the URL with fnv1a32, and `filterChannels` calls it once per MATCHING row to sort favourites first. So the bench hashed 6,877 URLs per keystroke that the shipped code has never hashed: `assign_ids` has run on every parse since the first commit and every cache this plugin has ever written carries `id` on every channel. Measured on the real 10,000-channel cache, Qt 6.11.2, 50 iterations: `a` **26.52 ms**, `e` 27.22, `s` 26.42, `t` 25.82 -- against 95.28, 91.28, 87.76 and 79.38 with `id` stripped. **3.6x**, and EVERY single-character query is inside the 30 ms budget. Profiled: matchRank over all 10,000 costs 17.7 ms and adding channelId per match takes it to 61.9, so 44 ms of the 70 was one call production never makes. The remedy this row recorded -- do not filter until the second keystroke -- is NOT TAKEN and its premise is withdrawn; it would have cost a real feature to fix a number that came from a test fixture. Hoisting matchRank's two loop-invariant string allocations was measured first and buys 1.11x, not built. CLAUDE.md rule 10 says a double must never be more FORGIVING; this one was HARSHER, which the rule does not name and which is worse for a MEASUREMENT -- a forgiving double hides a defect, a harsh one INVENTS one, and an invented defect gets paid for in product. Fixed structurally: `scripts/qa-filter-bench.py` generates a playlist, parses it with the shipping helper and measures THAT, refusing any channel list missing an unconditional field; `tests/test_filter_bench_fixture.py` imports the bench's field tuples and refusal predicate and runs them against a real parse, so the join is a call. It rejected my own field list on the first run (the parser writes `headers`). Four mutations, all red. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, performance 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| F-CAL-5 | P3 | calibration | **Every contrast figure this project has ever quoted is the maximum of a distribution nobody had measured.** The peak-pixel method takes the single most contrasting pixel in a glyph run, so it saturates: once one pixel is fully covered it returns the 8-bit arithmetic composite exactly and says nothing about the other several thousand. Measured properly for the first time on 2026-09-23, by solving the per-pixel coverage and weighting by ink mass: the footer status, whose headline number is 4.7093, has a MEDIAN of 3.9365 and puts **9 per cent** of its ink above 4.5; the group count reads 4.6489 and puts 18.5 per cent above. The control is what makes that mean something rather than being a property of antialiasing: full-opacity text puts **52 per cent** above the same threshold, three to six times more, so the metric separates a rung at the line from text that is comfortably legible. The dim rung D-RUNG-2 accepted puts **zero** above it, median 2.26 -- which is what that acceptance actually bought. WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, and the row is explicit because the temptation is real: these sites do not fail WCAG. SC 1.4.3 is defined on the SPECIFIED colours, a caption computing 4.71 passes by that definition, and every rung decision taken against it was taken correctly. What it says is that the headline number describes the specified composite and must never be read as describing the rendered stroke -- at 10 px the two differ by about 0.8 ratio points at the median. CONSEQUENCE, recorded rather than acted on: if the goal is compliance the rung is the lever and the work is done; if the goal is legibility the rung is the WEAKEST lever, because size and weight move the distribution and opacity only moves its endpoint. The instrument ships on the dev branch as a QA script and five rows are pinned with the control beside them. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, coverage distribution 2026-09-23  ACCEPTED 2026-09-23 as a standing property of the instrument, which is what it always was; it was filed open because it had only just been measured. Nothing further is owed: the instrument ships as a QA script, five rows are pinned with the full-opacity control beside them, three checks hold the relationships (the peak overstates every run, the control separates a rung at the line from comfortable text by three to six times, and the dim rung D-RUNG-2 accepted puts zero ink above the threshold), and `docs/CONTRAST-RULING.md` carries the reading rule as an amendment: a figure here is a MAXIMUM, never quote one finer than about 0.05, and when the goal is legibility rather than compliance reach for size or weight before opacity. No design decision is reopened by it -- WCAG SC 1.4.3 is defined on the specified colours and every rung that passes by that definition still passes. It stays as the note that stops the next person reading a peak as a lived experience. | accepted |
| F-CAL-3 | P3 | calibration | **D-RUNG-14's bold captions were verified in the wrong environment, and do not measurably help on the real display.** The four bold sites were measured under headless cage on 2026-09-22 at 6.2377 against a 6.2384 model - model accuracy, which is what the fix rested on. Measured on the user's actual session the same day, same theme, same code, the same sites read 5.4475 (group count), 5.4590 (Sources count), 5.6956 (footer status) and 5.8559 (footer hints). 5.45 is precisely what the REGULAR weight measured on this same display on 2026-09-21, so bold changed nothing the user can see. The bold is genuinely applied (four font.bold:true on the right elements in the installed file, verified) and a real Bold face exists and resolves (fc-match monospace:bold gives JetBrainsMonoNerdFont-Bold.ttf), so this is glyph rasterisation differing between headless cage and the live session, not a missing font. Corroborated by the control: the 2 px cursor MARK, a solid shape, measured 9.3561 in BOTH environments - identical - so compositing and colour are the same and only text differs. CONSEQUENCE for the fixture: contrast-calibration.json now MIXES environments - its 2026-09-21 rows are live measurements and its 2026-09-22 bold and recalibrated rows are headless - so it does not describe one rendering. Either re-measure everything live, or record the environment per row and stop comparing across them. D-RUNG-14's row should not keep claiming bold recovers the shortfall. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, live pass 2026-09-22 FIXED 2026-09-22, structurally rather than by a re-measure, because both options this row offered were wrong alone: re-measuring everything live would have discarded the cage rows, which are the only evidence that the difference is rasterisation and not the model, and recording the environment without changing how tolerance is DRAWN would have left cage rows certifying live decisions. So every row now carries `env` (live or cage); tolerance is drawn per (sizeClass, env) PAIR and a pair with no rows is refused rather than inheriting one it never earned; the optimism mean is computed per environment, live -0.376 against cage -0.048, nearly eight times, where the pooled -0.199 it replaces described neither rendering; and a size class with NO live row is refused outright, because cage certifies nothing a user can see. The live bold figures that refuted D-RUNG-14 existed only in prose and are now rows. So is the control, in both environments: a solid 2 px rule at the text token reads 9.3561 in each, so colour and compositing agree and only glyph coverage differs -- asserted with the compared-pair count returned so it cannot pass by comparing nothing. The live footer-hints figure (5.8559) is deliberately NOT a row, for the same reason its cage twin was left out: it is dimmed by `<font color>` inside `StyledText`, not by scene-graph opacity, so a 0.7 prediction would assert something false about how it is drawn. Six new checks, node 1464 to 1470; run against the fixture exactly as it shipped, TEN go red, including all six. Correction to this row's own text above: the cage bold pass was 2026-09-21 segment C, not 2026-09-22. SECOND FINDING, sharper than the first: the live bold pass ran on catppuccin, whose 0.7 caption rung already measured 5.45, above 4.5 before any change, so it measured the one theme that never failed. D-RUNG-14 is reopened on that. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, F-CAL-3 fix 2026-09-22 WITHDRAWN 2026-09-22, same day, by measurement on the real display: the diagnosis in this row is WRONG and so was the fix built on it. The two environments were never the variable. The live pass measured a shell that had NEVER LOADED the build it believed it was measuring -- `manifest.json` sets `keepLoaded: true`, so the overlay stays mounted across a hot reload, and `omarchy plugin update` ends in `rescanPlugins` rather than a restart, leaving the pre-bold component instantiated while the file on disk carried the bold. Proven from the journal, not argued: the boot the pass ran in logs Guide.qml warnings at lines 2670 and 2676, the 0.7.1 and 0.7.2 line numbers, and NEVER at 2823, which is where 0.7.3's MouseArea sits and where the current shell's warnings land. Re-measured on the user's own shell after the reboot that finally loaded 0.7.3, the footer status reads 6.2377 -- model accuracy -- where the pass recorded 5.6956. SECOND ERROR, mine, found by an adversarial pass and not by me: the peak-pixel statistic SATURATES. It is a MAX over a glyph run, so once one pixel is fully covered it returns the 8-bit composite exactly; its resolution is one LSB, about 0.05 ratio points, and it is blind by construction to hinting, subpixel positioning and scale. So it cannot establish that the environments differ AND cannot establish that they agree -- my own 'they render identically' is equally unsupported, and is not claimed. The withdrawn parts of the fix are recorded on F-CAL-4. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, F-CAL-3 withdrawal 2026-09-22 | **withdrawn** |
| F-CAL-4 | P2 | calibration | **A live pass measured a component the shell had never loaded, and nothing could have told it.** The verification that produced F-CAL-3's numbers ran against a resident overlay instantiated from the previous build; the file on disk had the change and the running QML did not. Every artefact looked right -- correct file, correct version in `manifest.json`, correct theme, the update command having reported success -- so the pass recorded three rows as live measurements of a bold caption when they were regular-weight renders of 0.7.2, and a whole tolerance split was built on them. This is the same failure shape as CLAUDE.md rule 13: `env` was provenance recorded as a NAME, and nothing checked what it claimed. FIXED by making it a call. Every calibration row now records the BUILD that rendered it and, where known, the background it actually measured; a bold row on a build known to lack the bold is refused; a row whose measured background is not the surface it names is refused. Three checks, each proven red by mutation, and the first of them is red against the exact rows committed that morning. The three bad rows are removed rather than relabelled, since their surface was never recorded either. Process rule adopted with it: **a live pass confirms the loaded build before reading a pixel** -- here that is one `journalctl` line matching a version-distinctive source line number, and it is free. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, F-CAL-3 withdrawal 2026-09-22 | verified fixed |
| D-HOST-2 | P2 | host | **D-HOST-1's notice has never been observed to fire, and one cause is proven.** 0.7.5 carried the check, with the right ladder order and an empty `savedSearches` above it, and it stayed SILENT through the real 0.7.5 to 0.7.7 update it exists for: the footer measured 180 px before and after, the width of the ordinary counts line, where the notice is 50 characters and would be about 300. PROVEN CAUSE for that failure: an update REPLACES the file rather than editing it. Measured on a scratch clone, a git fast-forward took `manifest.json` from inode 495084 to 495097, so the `FileView`'s `watchChanges` was bound to a file that no longer exists and never fired; `onDiskVersion` still held the version read at startup, equalled `PLUGIN_VERSION`, and `staleBuild` answered false. CANDIDATE FIX, and it is **UNVERIFIED**: `recheckBuild()` reloads the manifest from its PATH and the guide calls it on open, which is the moment the notice would be read. Two mutations catch it and the gate is green. What is missing is the thing that matters -- an end-to-end observation. A harness attempt replaced `manifest.json` with a different version, closed and reopened the guide, and the footer did not change, so either the fix does not work or the harness does not exercise this path, and I could not tell which from the outside. **The notice has therefore never been seen to fire in any environment.** That is the honest state and it is the same shape as the defect above: D-HOST-1 shipped on unit tests with no observation, and rule 14 is exactly about that. Next, and it is one measurement rather than more code: instrument the service to report `onDiskVersion` and `staleBuild` over IPC, so the question can be answered by reading a value instead of by inferring it from pixel widths. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, update pass 2026-09-23  VERIFIED FIXED 2026-09-23, and the notice has now been OBSERVED firing for the first time since D-HOST-1 shipped. The instrument was the problem: the only previous attempt to see it measured the pixel width of a footer line, which cannot distinguish "the fix failed" from "the harness does not exercise this". `statusSummary` now carries `build: {running, onDisk, stale}` -- what the loaded component believes it is, what is on disk beside it, and the verdict -- so the question is answered by reading a value. With that, the two halves separate cleanly. Replacing `manifest.json` (inode 170886 to 171018, a real replace) and reading WITHOUT reopening the guide: `onDisk` still 0.7.7, `stale` false -- **the `watchChanges` watcher does not fire on a replace, proven directly rather than inferred from inodes**. Closing and reopening the guide: `onDisk` 9.9.9, `stale` **true** -- `recheckBuild` works. And the footer closes the loop end to end: 162 px showing the counts before, **300 px** after, which is the predicted width of the 50-character notice. Three findings from one measurement, where two rounds of pixel-reading had produced none. Version strings carry no credential and no path, so the new field adds nothing to the redaction surface. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, update pass 2026-09-23 | verified fixed |
| D-HOST-1 | P2 | host | **`omarchy plugin update` does not make a QML change take effect, and says nothing.** The command fetches, fast-forwards and calls `omarchy-shell shell rescanPlugins`, which is a hot reload rather than a restart. With `keepLoaded: true` in `manifest.json` -- which this plugin sets, so its overlay survives reloads -- an already-mounted overlay keeps the component it was built from, so an interface change does not appear until the shell restarts or the user logs out. Observed here across two releases: the boot that updated to 0.7.3 at 14:58 logged Guide.qml warnings at the 0.7.2 line numbers for the rest of its life and never once at a 0.7.3 line number. The user-visible consequence is that someone who updates keeps running the old interface with no indication, including for a change made for accessibility. Recorded in CHANGELOG.md so users are told to restart. NOT yet raised upstream and not yet reproduced deliberately: `Qt.clearComponentCache()` is called by the host and evidently does not take here, and why is hypothesis. Next: reproduce on a trivial plugin edit, then decide between an upstream report and a plugin-side version banner. docs/QA-RESULTS.md, F-CAL-3 withdrawal 2026-09-22 FIXED 2026-09-23, our half of it. The plugin can tell that it is stale, and now says so. `Model.PLUGIN_VERSION` is compiled into the QML and therefore travels with the COMPONENT; the version in `manifest.json` travels with the DIRECTORY, which is what an update rewrites. Service reads its own manifest through `Qt.resolvedUrl`, so it resolves against the running component rather than a guessed path, and compares. When they disagree the guide footer reads `Updated - restart the shell to see the new version`, placed BELOW a provider warning (which is more urgent) and ABOVE the plain counts (which is what the footer shows most of the time), so it is seen without ever displacing something that needs acting on first. The comparison is only meaningful if the two agree when a version is cut, so the release gate now refuses a tree where they differ or where the constant is missing -- proven both ways, and it immediately caught a real one when the release tests bumped a manifest without the constant. `manifestVersion` answers empty rather than throwing on a half-written file, because an update rewrites the directory under a running process and a mid-write read is ordinary. `staleBuild` answers false whenever either side is unknown: a notice nobody can act on is worse than no notice. Five checks, seven mutations, each caught by exactly one. STILL OPEN UPSTREAM and deliberately not filed without asking: the host calls `Qt.clearComponentCache()` and it evidently does not take for a `keepLoaded` overlay, which is the actual bug and is not ours to fix. Our notice is a workaround that tells the user what to do, not a repair. | fixed |

Gates green (681 node, 199 python, 33 qml, validate 0 on the repo and on the installed clone). The machine was restored byte for byte and proved. **0 open P1/P2: go for v0.2.1.**

M2-02 player pass on 8f9447e (QA, 2026-09-14, `docs/QA-RESULTS.md` section "M2-02 player pass on 8f9447e"): the `docs/QA-PLAYER.md` runbook executed on the reference machine with the installed clone moved to the RC and back. **96 of 129 `PLY-` cases pass, 5 fail, 6 pass with a corrected expectation, 6 blocked, 16 not run**; seven defects filed, **one P1**. The milestone's whole reason to exist is proven: `omarchy restart shell` with a channel playing keeps the **same mpv pid and the same `/proc` start time**, exactly one window at every 100 ms sample, `time-pos` advancing with no reload, and the full identity including `launchedFrom` recovered from the player's own stash - six clean runs, ten consecutive restarts, and a `pkill -KILL`ed shell that ran no teardown at all. S-03 is closed on the first channel and byte-identically after ten zaps; headers reach mpv only over the socket and are cleared between channels; 241,675 `/proc` samples carry 0 plugin-originated needles; the journal carries 0 mpv lines. Spike caveats C1-C10 are implemented as prescribed and the reattach-after-death trap is unreproducible (750 ms). PO-2's `disable` gate passes at 6676 ms and the README's escape hatch stops an orphan in 220 ms with no shell running. PO-3's full procedure marks silently with **zero** toasts and the guide draws the alert glyph U+F0026 and `Failed HH:MM - Space to retry`, not a red row. The superseded-stop regression is closed and the stop ladder completes with **no shell alive**. Every performance budget is met except the wedged-stop one. Blocking: **D-PLY-1** (P1, the interface goes idle while a respawned player keeps playing, from two independent triggers) and **D-PLY-2** (PO-2's `remove` case, which also deletes the documented remedy). The ARCH-P section 15 race was gated as instructed and **reproduces at 47% (14/30)** - D-PLY-4. Harness rule-11 evidence: 49/49 at `8f9447e`, 33/49 against pre-M2-02 `5e90283`. Gates green (885 node, 247 python, 42 qml, validate 0). The machine was restored byte for byte and proved. **No-go for v0.3.0.**

M2-02 fix verification on b16b479 (QA, 2026-09-14, `docs/QA-RESULTS.md` section "M2-02 fix verification on b16b479"): the seven `D-PLY-*` defects re-verified against their original reproductions, with the installed clone moved to the RC and back. **All seven discharged**: D-PLY-1 (P1) does not reproduce from either trigger (A 3/3, B 2/2) and QA counted the journal's relaunch line directly to confirm **exactly one relaunch, never two**, because the harness check for that clause never runs (D-PLY-9); D-PLY-3 is clean 3 of 3 where it failed 2 of 3; **D-PLY-4 re-measured with QA's own unchanged `race-trial.sh`: 30 trials, 30 won, 0 lost**, against 14 lost in 30 at `8f9447e`; D-PLY-5's warning reaches the guide's existing warning line without refusing the option; D-PLY-6's wedged reap is **4236-4263 ms** against the 4500 ms budget, and a controlled A/B with only the helper swapped gives 6187 ms at `8f9447e` vs 4177 ms here; D-PLY-7's screenshots and resume positions land at the documented paths at **mode 600** with **nothing in `$HOME`**; and D-PLY-2 is closed as accepted under PO-8 with the README's recovery tested verbatim, including proof that the pattern's leading `^mpv ` anchor is load-bearing (without it the pattern matches the shell pasting it). Gates green (954 node, 260 python, 47 qml, validate 0); harness 75/0 (now 83; the repaired check plus positive controls) at `b16b479` and 60/20 at `396a69a`, satisfying rule 11 for the four new scenarios. The six corrected expectations still behave as corrected, and PLY-RST-11's focus consequence is now **run** rather than skipped. Privacy: 20,602 `/proc` sweeps and **2,199,888 process samples with 0 plugin-originated needles**; the three disclosed residuals and nothing else; the new option-warning and directories add no surface. Containment: every artifact `700`/`600`, nothing in `$HOME`; the one file outside the documented list is mpv's content-free shader cache (D-PLY-10). Four new P3s (D-PLY-8 pre-existing socket residue, D-PLY-9 test tooling, D-PLY-10 docs, D-PLY-11 a once-seen divergence under a trigger the guide cannot produce), **none gating**. The machine was restored byte for byte and proved. **Go for v0.3.0.**

| 2026-09-26 | **0.8.0 released, and a finding about every release since the listing.** `release.py build` on `dev` (65978e4) -> `main` ef05ff6d9c9a06fa771f91e6c84a8fda2380ae51, parent 9809428, tag v0.8.0, fast-forward, 13 files. Contents: M2-13 the channel wall, `cache logos-clear` (D-LOGO-9), the symlink guard (D-LOGO-10) and D-LOGO-8's streaming logos. Five adversarial preflights, every one of which blocked the release, and **every blocker was a claim rather than a crash** -- the guide did not open in the list as the CHANGELOG said; the footer described arrow keys the search-mode handler did not implement; the release was framed as \"a wall of pictures\" when logos are off by default; \"about twice as many channels\" measured 1.33x at best and 1.00x in a default shape. **Three of those four were introduced by the previous pass's own repair.** Verification filed as omacom/omarchy-plugin-marketplace#8826, validated and security-baseline passed at `ef05ff6`; #8594 (0.7.10) closed as superseded. **THE FINDING: the marketplace has never served anything but v0.7.1.** Only the original listing #7374 ever carried `approved-and-verified` and `listed`. #7936 (0.7.2), #8105 (0.7.3), #8438 (0.7.9) and #8594 (0.7.10) each reached `validated` with a passing baseline and were then superseded and closed `NOT_PLANNED` without a maintainer ever applying `approved-and-verified`. So the verified snapshot is still `7499ff3`, and four releases of work have reached nobody who installs from the marketplace. **CAUSE, and it is ours.** The first hypothesis was the second acknowledgment, left unchecked on all four; #8826 checks it, because it is true of this plugin. But `VERIFICATION.md` does not make that box a gate -- it says only that \"a write-authorized maintainer may apply `approved-and-verified` only after the current bot-authored reports are available\". The reports were available on all four. What actually distinguishes them is TIME: the one successful verification, listing #7374, took **3.5 days** from open to approved. The four update requests were open for **1.2, 1.5, 1.0 and 1.5 days** and were then closed -- by us, superseding each one with the next release. We shipped eight tags in four days and closed our own pending request every time, at roughly a third of the maintainers' only demonstrated turnaround. The marketplace never had a chance to action any of them. NOT a maintainer-side problem and nothing to ask them: #8826 stays OPEN until it is actioned or explicitly refused, and a future release does not close it -- a pending request blocks nothing, since `VERIFICATION.md` states the existing snapshot remains unchanged while an update is pending. |

D-PAUSE-1 (pause surviving a channel change, so a zap loaded the new channel paused while every surface said playing) and D-ZAP-2 (the changelog promising a skip message that no shipping code ever showed) were both blockers, and D-ZAP-2 would have shipped the false sentence into every install because CHANGELOG.md is on the allowlist. The D-SINK-4 changelog entry also understated its own cost: `--load-scripts=no` disables every autoloaded mpv script, the user's own included, not just mpv-mpris. | 2026-09-24 | **Dead-channel memory: R8, R11 and UX ruling 9 amended, and the store is the CACHE rather than `state.json`.** PO asked for failure marks to survive a restart; R8 and R11 (ARCHITECTURE.md section 12) and UX ruling 9 (UX.md) all specified session-only, so they are amended in place rather than contradicted silently. PLAN.md:240 requires this entry before the code. THE STORE IS `~/.cache/omarchy-iptv/sources/<key>/failed.json`, NOT state.json, and that decision is load-bearing: a design review found two blockers in the state.json version. (1) Channel ids are global across sources (ARCHITECTURE-SOURCES D14), so a mark earned on one provider would paint a DIFFERENT provider's working channel as failed. (2) A map keyed by channel id goes stale on an id rotation, and `remap_state_ids` moves only favorites/recents/lastPlayed/session -- D-ID-1's board row says in as many words that the failed map is outside its blast radius BECAUSE it is session-only, so persisting it in state.json would silently reopen a verified-fixed P2. The cache store dissolves both: per-source by construction, removed with the source (the CACHE_FILES path D-LOGO-4 already taught), and a mark whose channel id is absent from the current channels.json is DROPPED ON LOAD, which self-heals id rotation, provider changes and channel removal with no remapper work. It also avoids the four state whitelists, the version question and the `cloneState` patch-loop trap (a patched key survives one clone and is dropped by the next, which passes every test written at the call site). Shape: a capped ARRAY of {id, at} newest-first, because the savedSearches precedent used an array for deterministic eviction order; epoch seconds, not the display string. TTL and cap applied at a named site with an injected clock, never inside a parser, so the shared fixtures stay clock-free. Value type changes from `"HH:MM"` to epoch, which six display sinks must be taught. | PO |

row two hundred lines above says fixed under ruling SG1 with twenty node checks
and all six mutants caught.

product owner on 2026-09-16 for phases 1 through 3. `docs/PLAN-NEXT.md` is
